NODES

--- OTOLOGY/NEUROTOLOGY (121) ---
Annular Ligament (Stapediovestibular) [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · ligament · temporal-bone · midline · draft] — Ring of elastic fibrous tissue anchoring the stapes footplate within the oval window; its ossification/fixation is the hallmark of otosclerosis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Anterior Malleal Ligament [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · ligament · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Suspensory ligament running from the malleus neck (anterior process) to the petrotympanic (Glaserian) fissure; contributes to the ossicular rotational axis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Antihelix [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Y-shaped ridge anterior and parallel to the helix; superiorly it divides into superior and inferior crura that bound the triangular fossa. Loss of its fold produces prominent-ear deformity.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka anthelix}
Antitragus [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Small tubercle opposite the tragus, superior to the lobule; separated from the tragus by the intertragic notch and continuous with the inferior antihelix.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Auricular Cartilage [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Single elastic-cartilage framework of the auricle whose folds and hollows form the surface subunits (helix, antihelix, tragus, concha); continuous medially with the cartilaginous external auditory canal. The lobule lacks cartilage.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka pinna cartilage, auricular framework}
Cerebellopontine Angle [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · space · skull-base · paired · draft] — CSF-filled space between pons, cerebellum and petrous temporal bone. Contains CN VII/VIII, AICA and the flocculus; most common site of extra-axial posterior fossa tumors (vestibular schwannoma, meningioma, epidermoid).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CPA}
Cochlea [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — 2.5 turns spiral. Contains scala vestibuli, scala media (cochlear duct), and scala tympani. Organ of Corti sits on basilar membrane in scala media. Tonotopic: base=high freq, apex=low freq.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka bony cochlea}
Concha [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Deepest hollow of the auricle, divided by the crus of the helix into cymba and cavum; the cavum concha leads medially into the external auditory canal.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka concha auriculae, cymba concha, cavum concha}
Epitympanum [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · space · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Superior portion of middle ear (attic). Contains head of malleus and body of incus. Cholesteatoma commonly originates from pars flaccida retraction into epitympanum.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka attic}
Eustachian Tube [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · space · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Connects middle ear to nasopharynx. Adults: 36mm, 45° angle. Children shorter, more horizontal. Equalizes middle ear pressure. Lined by respiratory epithelium.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka auditory tube, pharyngotympanic tube}
External Auditory Canal [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · space · external-ear · paired · vetted] — S-shaped canal ~2.5cm. Lateral 1/3 cartilaginous, medial 2/3 bony. Isthmus is the narrowest point at bone-cartilage junction.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka ear canal, EAC, external acoustic meatus}
Facial Recess [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · space · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Triangular space bounded by: fossa incudis superiorly, facial nerve medially, chorda tympani laterally. Key surgical landmark for cochlear implant electrode insertion.  (Cummings 7e)
Fissula Ante Fenestram [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · space · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Cleft of embryonic connective tissue just anterior to the oval window; the most common site of the initial otosclerotic focus.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Geniculate Ganglion [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Sensory ganglion of CN VII at first genu. Gives off greater superficial petrosal nerve. Site of herpes zoster reactivation in Ramsay Hunt syndrome.  (Cummings 7e)
Helix [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Curved outer rim of the auricle, beginning at the crus of the helix within the concha and ending at the lobule. An elastic-cartilage subunit of the auricular framework.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Incudomallear Joint [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · joint · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Saddle-shaped synovial joint between the malleus head and incus body; the two move as a unit about the anteroposterior ligamentous axis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Incudostapedial Joint [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · joint · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Ball-and-socket synovial joint between the long process (lenticular) of the incus and the stapes head; commonly disarticulated in ossiculoplasty and most eroded ossicular link in chronic OM.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Incus [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Articulates with malleus (body) and stapes (lenticular process). Long process is most vulnerable to necrosis in chronic otitis media.  (Cummings 7e)
Inferior Crus of Antihelix [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Lower, more horizontal limb of the antihelix; with the superior crus it bounds the triangular fossa.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka inferior crus}
Internal Auditory Canal [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · foramen · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Contains CN VII, CN VIII (superior/inferior vestibular, cochlear), and labyrinthine artery. Transverse and vertical crests divide IAC into four quadrants.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka IAC, internal acoustic meatus}
Lateral Malleal Ligament [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · ligament · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Fan-shaped ligament from the malleus neck to the tympanic notch (of Rivinus), forming the roof of Prussak's space.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Lateral Semicircular Canal [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Key landmark in mastoid surgery — lies medial to antrum. Oriented 30° above horizontal. Most commonly involved in BPPV is posterior canal, not lateral.  (Cummings 7e)
Lobule (Earlobe) [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · external-ear · paired · draft] — Inferior fibrofatty portion of the auricle; unlike the other subunits it contains NO cartilage, only fibrous tissue, fat, and skin. Continuous superiorly with the antitragus and helix.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka earlobe, lobulus auriculae}
Malleus [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Largest ossicle. Manubrium attached to TM. Head articulates with incus in epitympanum. Lateral process creates malleolar fold.  (Cummings 7e)
Mastoid [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Pneumatized portion of temporal bone. Contains mastoid antrum connecting to epitympanum via aditus ad antrum. Degree of pneumatization varies.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka mastoid process}
Mastoid Part of Temporal Bone [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Posteroinferior part of the temporal bone bearing the mastoid process and air cells; continuous with the petrous part and the site of the mastoid antrum communicating with the epitympanum.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka mastoid portion}
Organ of Corti [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Sensory epithelium in scala media. Inner hair cells (single row, ~3500) are primary transducers. Outer hair cells (3 rows, ~12000) provide cochlear amplification.  (Cummings 7e)
Otic Capsule [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Dense endochondral bone of the petrous temporal bone that encloses the membranous labyrinth; contains the cochlea, vestibule, and semicircular canals. Its enchondral layer does not remodel, relevant in otosclerosis and otic-capsule-violating fractures.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka bony labyrinth capsule}
Oval Window [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · foramen · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Niche between middle and inner ear, sealed by stapes footplate and annular ligament. Transmits sound to scala vestibuli.  (Cummings 7e)
Petrosquamous Suture [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · joint · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Suture between the petrous and squamous parts of the temporal bone; a persistent (Koerner's) septum along this line can complicate mastoid surgery and provide a route for infection spread.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Koerner's septum}
Petrotympanic Fissure (Glaserian Fissure) [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · joint · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Slit between the petrous and tympanic parts at the medial mandibular fossa. Transmits the chorda tympani nerve out of the middle ear and the anterior tympanic artery; also transmits the anterior malleal ligament.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Glaserian fissure, glaserian fissure}
Petrous Part of Temporal Bone [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Dense pyramidal portion of the temporal bone housing the otic capsule (cochlea, vestibule, semicircular canals) and the internal auditory canal; wedged between the sphenoid and occipital bones at the skull base.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka petrous pyramid, petrous temporal bone}
Posterior Incudal Ligament [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · ligament · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Suspends the short process of the incus in the incudal fossa; a key fulcrum, relevant when disarticulating the incudostapedial joint.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Promontory [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Rounded elevation on medial wall of middle ear formed by basal turn of cochlea. Jacobson nerve (CN IX tympanic branch) courses over it.  (Cummings 7e)
Round Window [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · foramen · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Membrane-covered opening of scala tympani. Serves as pressure release for fluid displaced by stapes footplate movement. Important CI electrode entry.  (Cummings 7e)
Scaphoid Fossa [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Narrow curved gutter between the helix and the antihelix; a hollow of the auricular cartilage framework.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka scapha}
Semicircular Canals [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Three canals (superior, posterior, lateral) detect angular acceleration. Each has ampulla with crista ampullaris. Lateral canal 30° from horizontal.  (Cummings 7e)
Sinus Tympani [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · space · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Deep recess posteromedial to round window niche, medial to facial nerve. Difficult to visualize directly — common site for residual cholesteatoma.  (Cummings 7e)
Squamous Part of Temporal Bone [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Thin, flat superolateral plate of the temporal bone; bears the mandibular fossa and the zygomatic process, forming the roof and part of the wall of the external auditory canal.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka squama temporalis, temporal squama}
Stapedius Muscle [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · muscle · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Smallest skeletal muscle. Innervated by CN VII. Contracts reflexively to loud sound (acoustic reflex), dampening ossicular chain movement.  (Cummings 7e)
Stapes [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Smallest bone in body. Footplate sits in oval window. Stapedius muscle attaches to neck/head, dampening ossicular chain vibration.  (Cummings 7e)
Stylomastoid Foramen [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · foramen · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Exit point of CN VII from temporal bone. Landmark for facial nerve identification in parotid surgery. Located between styloid and mastoid processes.  (Cummings 7e)
Superior Crus of Antihelix [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Upper of the two divergent limbs of the antihelix; with the inferior crus it bounds the triangular fossa.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka superior crus}
Superior Malleal Ligament [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · ligament · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Suspends the malleus head from the epitympanic roof (tegmen); helps define the axis of ossicular rotation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Tegmen Tympani [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Thin bone forming roof of middle ear and mastoid. Separates middle ear from middle cranial fossa. Erosion risks meningitis and CSF leak.  (Cummings 7e)
Temporal Bone [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Paired bone of the skull base composed of squamous, petrous, mastoid, and tympanic parts plus the styloid process; houses the middle and inner ear and transmits the facial and vestibulocochlear nerves.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)
Tensor Tympani [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · muscle · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Innervated by CN V3. Inserts on manubrium of malleus. Tenses TM and dampens sound. Located in semicanal above eustachian tube.  (Cummings 7e)
Tragus [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Cartilaginous projection anterior to the external auditory meatus, overlapping its opening; separated from the antitragus by the intertragic notch.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Triangular Fossa [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · cartilage · external-ear · paired · draft] — Depression bounded by the superior and inferior crura of the antihelix; a hollow of the auricular cartilage framework.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka fossa triangularis}
Tympanic Annulus (Annulus Fibrosus) [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · ligament · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Fibrocartilaginous ring seating the pars tensa in the tympanic sulcus; deficient superiorly at the notch of Rivinus where the pars flaccida attaches.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Tympanic Membrane [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · membrane · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Three layers: squamous epithelium, fibrous middle, mucosal lining. Pars tensa (inferior) and pars flaccida (superior, Shrapnell membrane).  (Cummings 7e)  {aka eardrum, TM}
Tympanic Part of Temporal Bone [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Curved plate forming the anterior wall, floor, and lower posterior wall of the bony external auditory canal and the tympanic sulcus for the tympanic annulus; ends medially at the petrotympanic fissure.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka tympanic plate}
Tympanosquamous Fissure [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · joint · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Suture line on the mandibular fossa between the squamous and tympanic parts of the temporal bone; lies anterolateral to the petrotympanic fissure.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka squamotympanic fissure}
Vestibule [anatomy · Otology/Neurotology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Central chamber of bony labyrinth. Contains utricle and saccule (otolith organs for linear acceleration). Oval window opens into vestibule.  (Cummings 7e)
Canal-Wall-Up vs Canal-Wall-Down Mastoidectomy [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Cholesteatoma surgery decision: canal-wall-up preserves ear canal anatomy but has higher residual/recurrent disease and needs second-look; canal-wall-down exteriorizes disease into a mastoid bowl with lower recurrence but bowl maintenance.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CWU vs CWD}
Carhart Notch [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Artifactual depression of bone-conduction thresholds maximal at 2 kHz seen in otosclerosis due to loss of the ossicular resonance contribution; it typically resolves after successful stapes surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cochlear Implant Candidacy [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Bilateral moderate-to-profound SNHL with limited benefit from hearing aids (aided sentence scores below criterion, e.g., AzBio). FDA indications expanded to single-sided deafness (4PTA >80 dB impaired ear, ≤30 dB contralateral) after a CROS trial.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9592177/)  {aka CI candidacy}
Cochlear Implant Electrode Array [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Intracochlear electrode inserted through the round window/cochleostomy into scala tympani. Lateral-wall (straight) arrays favor atraumatic hearing preservation; perimodiolar (modiolus-hugging) arrays place contacts closer to the spiral ganglion.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka electrode array}
Conductive Hearing Loss [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Hearing loss from impaired sound transmission through the external/middle ear, producing an air-bone gap and a negative Rinne. Causes include cerumen, effusion, TM perforation, ossicular fixation/discontinuity and otosclerosis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CHL, air-bone gap}
Endolymphatic Hydrops [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Distension of the endolymphatic (scala media) compartment underlying Ménière's disease, attributed to impaired endolymph resorption. Can be visualized on delayed intravenous/intratympanic gadolinium MRI.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka hydrops}
Koos Grading of Vestibular Schwannoma [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Grades vestibular schwannoma I-IV by extension: I intracanalicular, II small CPA, III CPA reaching brainstem, IV brainstem compression/displacement. Guides observation vs treatment.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Koos grade}
Ossicular Prosthesis (PORP/TORP) [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Reconstructs the ossicular chain: a partial prosthesis (PORP) bridges the TM/malleus to the stapes head when the superstructure is intact; a total prosthesis (TORP) reaches the footplate when the stapes superstructure is absent.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka PORP, TORP}
Third Window Physiology [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — A pathologic third mobile window (e.g., SSCD, large vestibular aqueduct) shunts acoustic energy away from the cochlea, lowering air-conduction (apparent air-bone gap) while paradoxically improving bone conduction to negative thresholds.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka third mobile window}
Tympanic Membrane Graft [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Grafting material for eardrum repair: temporalis fascia, tragal/conchal cartilage-perichondrium (preferred for high-risk/revision ears). Placed by underlay (medial) or overlay (lateral) technique.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka fascia graft}
Weber & Rinne Tuning Fork Tests [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — 512 Hz bedside tests: Weber lateralizes to the poorer ear in SNHL and to the affected ear in conductive loss; a negative Rinne (BC>AC) indicates a conductive loss of roughly ≥25 dB.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka tuning fork tests}
Wullstein Tympanoplasty Classification [concept · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Types I-V describe the reconstructed sound-conducting mechanism: Type I (myringoplasty, intact ossicles) through Types II-IV (graft onto remaining ossicles/footplate) and Type V (fenestration).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Intratympanic Gentamicin [drug · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Vestibulotoxic aminoglycoside instilled transtympanically as a 'chemical labyrinthectomy' for intractable Ménière's vertigo, ablating vestibular hair cells while attempting to spare hearing.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka IT gentamicin}
Medical Therapy for Ménière's (Diuretic/Betahistine) [drug · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — First-line conservative management: low-salt diet, thiazide/triamterene diuretic, and betahistine (widely used outside the US), with vestibular suppressants for acute attacks.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka betahistine}
Systemic Corticosteroids (SSNHL) [drug · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — High-dose oral prednisone (or equivalent) is the initial option for idiopathic SSNHL, ideally started within 2 weeks of onset. AAO-HNSF 2019 lists it as an option (not mandate) given weak evidence.  (https://www.entnet.org/quality-practice/quality-products/clinical-practice-guidelines/sudden-hearing-loss-update/)  {aka prednisone}
Chorda Tympani [nerve · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Branch of CN VII. Carries taste from anterior 2/3 tongue and parasympathetic fibers to submandibular/sublingual glands. Crosses middle ear between malleus and incus.  (Cummings 7e)
Facial Nerve (CN VII) [nerve · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Complex course: IAC → labyrinthine → geniculate ganglion → tympanic → second genu → mastoid → stylomastoid foramen. Labyrinthine segment is narrowest and most susceptible to injury.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka CN VII}
Greater Superficial Petrosal Nerve [nerve · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Branch of CN VII at geniculate ganglion. Carries parasympathetic fibers for lacrimal gland secretion. Exits via hiatus of facial canal.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka GSPN}
Jacobson Nerve (Tympanic Branch CN IX) [nerve · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Branch of CN IX. Courses over promontory forming tympanic plexus. Provides sensation to middle ear mucosa and parasympathetic fibers to parotid (via lesser petrosal nerve).  (Cummings 7e)
Vestibulocochlear Nerve (CN VIII) [nerve · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Travels through IAC with CN VII. Superior and inferior vestibular divisions plus cochlear division. Vulnerable to vestibular schwannoma.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka CN VIII}
Acute Mastoiditis [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Suppurative infection of mastoid air cells complicating acute OM, with postauricular erythema/swelling, protrusion of the pinna, and loss of the postauricular crease. Coalescent mastoiditis requires IV antibiotics +/- mastoidectomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Benign Paroxysmal Positional Vertigo [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Brief positional vertigo from displaced otoconia, most often canalithiasis of the posterior semicircular canal. Diagnosed by provocative positional testing; highly responsive to canalith repositioning.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka BPPV}
Cholesteatoma [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Keratinizing squamous epithelium in middle ear/mastoid. Acquired (pars flaccida retraction) more common than congenital. Erodes bone via enzymatic activity and pressure.  (Cummings 7e)
Chronic Otitis Media [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Permanent TM perforation with chronic middle ear drainage >6 weeks. May be with or without cholesteatoma. Often causes conductive hearing loss.  (Cummings 7e)
Congenital Cholesteatoma [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — White keratin mass behind an intact tympanic membrane, classically anterosuperior quadrant, without prior perforation/otorrhea history. Arises from epithelial rests (Levenson criteria).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
CSF Otorrhea [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — CSF leak through a tegmen or otic-capsule breach, presenting as clear otorrhea (or rhinorrhea via the eustachian tube). Confirmed with beta-2 transferrin; most post-traumatic leaks resolve conservatively, with surgery for persistence.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CSF leak}
Eustachian Tube Dysfunction [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Failure of eustachian tube pressure equalization producing negative middle ear pressure, effusion, retraction and predisposition to chronic OM/cholesteatoma. Dilatory dysfunction may be treated with balloon dilation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ETD}
Hemotympanum [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Blood in the middle ear space behind an intact tympanic membrane, giving a bluish drum and a common early sign of temporal bone fracture. Usually resolves spontaneously.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ménière's Disease [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Idiopathic endolymphatic hydrops. Bárány/AAO-HNS 2015 definite criteria: ≥2 spontaneous vertigo episodes 20 min–12 h, audiometrically documented low-to-mid frequency SNHL in the affected ear, and fluctuating aural symptoms (hearing, tinnitus, fullness).  (https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.3233/VES-150549)  {aka Meniere disease, idiopathic endolymphatic hydrops}
Neurofibromatosis Type 2 [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Autosomal dominant tumor syndrome from biallelic loss of NF2 (merlin/schwannomin, chromosome 22q12). Hallmark is bilateral vestibular schwannomas; also meningiomas, ependymomas. Diagnosed by Manchester criteria.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka NF2}
Otogenic Facial Nerve Palsy [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Facial paresis complicating acute or chronic OM/cholesteatoma via a dehiscent tympanic segment of the fallopian canal. Acute OM cause is treated medically with myringotomy; cholesteatoma requires surgical decompression.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Otogenic Intracranial Abscess / Meningitis [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Intracranial spread of otitis media producing meningitis (most common), or temporal lobe/cerebellar abscess and epidural/subdural empyema. Requires urgent imaging, neurosurgery, and mastoid source control.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Otosclerosis [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Abnormal bone remodeling at oval window causing stapes fixation. Autosomal dominant with incomplete penetrance. Presents as progressive conductive hearing loss.  (Cummings 7e)
Petrous Apicitis [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Infection of pneumatized petrous apex air cells. Gradenigo triad: deep retro-orbital pain (CN V), abducens palsy/diplopia (CN VI in Dorello canal), and persistent otorrhea.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka apical petrositis}
Sensorineural Hearing Loss [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Hearing loss from cochlear hair-cell or cochlear-nerve (retrocochlear) dysfunction, with no air-bone gap. Etiologies include presbycusis, noise, ototoxicity and genetic loss; severe-to-profound cases are candidates for cochlear implantation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SNHL}
Sigmoid (Lateral) Sinus Thrombosis [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Otogenic thrombophlebitis of the sigmoid/lateral sinus with spiking 'picket-fence' fevers and Griesinger sign (postauricular edema over the mastoid emissary vein). Managed with antibiotics, surgery, and often anticoagulation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka lateral sinus thrombosis}
Subperiosteal / Bezold Abscess [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Pus escaping coalescent mastoiditis: postauricular subperiosteal abscess, or a Bezold abscess tracking through the mastoid tip into the sternocleidomastoid/neck.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Bezold abscess}
Sudden Sensorineural Hearing Loss [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — ≥30 dB SNHL over ≥3 contiguous frequencies within 72 hours; most idiopathic (ISSNHL). AAO-HNSF 2019 CPG: obtain audiogram, do NOT routinely order CT/labs, exclude retrocochlear disease with MRI, and counsel that early treatment improves odds.  (https://www.entnet.org/quality-practice/quality-products/clinical-practice-guidelines/sudden-hearing-loss-update/)  {aka SSNHL, ISSNHL}
Superior Semicircular Canal Dehiscence [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Absence of bone over the superior semicircular canal in the middle cranial fossa floor creating a mobile third window. Presents with sound-/pressure-induced vertigo (Tullio, Hennebert), autophony, pulsatile oscillopsia and a low-frequency conductive loss with intact acoustic reflexes.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5408023/)  {aka SSCD, SCDS}
Temporal Bone Fracture [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Classified as otic-capsule-sparing vs otic-capsule-violating (better predictor of facial palsy, SNHL and CSF leak than the older longitudinal/transverse scheme). Longitudinal fractures more often cause conductive loss; transverse cause SNHL/facial palsy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka temporal bone trauma}
Traumatic Facial Nerve Paralysis [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Injury most often at the perigeniculate region. Immediate-onset complete paralysis with >90-95% degeneration on ENoG (and absent voluntary EMG) favors surgical decompression; delayed or incomplete paralysis is observed with steroids and usually recovers.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Tympanic Membrane Perforation [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Defect in the eardrum from trauma or chronic OM. Central perforations are 'safe'; marginal/attic perforations are 'unsafe' owing to cholesteatoma risk. Causes conductive hearing loss proportional to size.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka TM perforation}
Tympanic Membrane Retraction Pocket [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Invagination of the pars flaccida or tensa driven by eustachian tube dysfunction/negative middle ear pressure. When keratin fails to clear, it evolves into acquired cholesteatoma.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vestibular Schwannoma [pathology · Otology/Neurotology · skull-base · vetted] — Benign tumor of Schwann cells on CN VIII (usually superior vestibular division). Most common CPA tumor. Bilateral = NF2. Presents with asymmetric SNHL.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka acoustic neuroma, VS}
Cochlear Implant [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Electrode array inserted via round window or cochleostomy into scala tympani. Posterior tympanotomy (facial recess) approach. For severe-profound SNHL.  (Cummings 7e)  {aka CI}
Cochlear Implant Mapping (Programming) [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Post-activation audiologic programming that sets threshold (T) and comfort (C/M) levels per electrode, selects the processing strategy, and drives auditory rehabilitation for optimal speech perception.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka mapping}
Dix-Hallpike Maneuver [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Gold-standard provocative test for posterior canal BPPV; a positive test shows latency-limited, fatigable upbeating-torsional nystagmus toward the dependent (affected) ear.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Dix-Hallpike}
Electrocochleography [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Records cochlear summating (SP) and action (AP) potentials; an elevated SP/AP ratio (>0.4-0.5) supports endolymphatic hydrops in Ménière's disease, though sensitivity is limited.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ECochG}
Endolymphatic Sac Decompression/Shunt [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Non-ablative, hearing-preserving surgery for medically refractory Ménière's disease; the mastoid bone over the endolymphatic sac is decompressed +/- shunted. Efficacy beyond placebo remains debated.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ELS decompression}
Epley Canalith Repositioning [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Sequential head positioning that moves otoconia out of the posterior semicircular canal back into the utricle; first-line, highly effective treatment for posterior canal BPPV.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka canalith repositioning procedure, CRP}
Intratympanic Steroid Injection [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Transtympanic dexamethasone/methylprednisolone diffusing across the round window. AAO-HNSF 2019 recommends offering it as SALVAGE therapy for incomplete recovery 2-6 weeks after onset; also used as initial therapy when systemic steroids are contraindicated.  (https://www.entnet.org/quality-practice/quality-products/clinical-practice-guidelines/sudden-hearing-loss-update/)  {aka IT steroids}
Mastoidectomy [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Canal wall up (CWU) vs canal wall down (CWD). Landmarks: tegmen, sigmoid, lateral SCC, facial nerve. CWD creates mastoid bowl requiring lifelong care.  (Cummings 7e)
Middle Cranial Fossa Approach [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Extradural approach over the tegmen for hearing preservation in small, predominantly intracanalicular vestibular schwannomas. The facial nerve lies superiorly, increasing exposure-related risk.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka middle fossa}
Myringoplasty [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Repair of a tympanic membrane perforation without addressing the middle ear or ossicular chain (Wullstein Type I). Suitable for a dry central perforation with a healthy ossicular chain.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ossiculoplasty [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Reconstruction of ossicular chain. PORP (partial) replaces incus, TORP (total) replaces all. Materials: titanium, hydroxyapatite, autograft.  (Cummings 7e)
Retrosigmoid (Suboccipital) Approach [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Posterior fossa approach for vestibular schwannoma allowing hearing preservation for tumors with limited lateral IAC extension. Risks include cerebellar retraction and headache.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka suboccipital}
Stapedectomy [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Removal of stapes and placement of prosthesis for otosclerosis. Stapedotomy (small fenestra) preferred. Risk: SNHL, vertigo, facial nerve injury, perilymph fistula.  (Cummings 7e)
Stapedotomy [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Preferred stapes surgery for otosclerosis: a small fenestra is made in the fixed footplate and a piston prosthesis is crimped from the incus, restoring ossicular mobility. Lower risk of SNHL than total stapedectomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka small fenestra stapedotomy}
Stereotactic Radiosurgery [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Single-session focused radiation (e.g., Gamma Knife) for vestibular schwannomas generally <2.5-3 cm, giving ~90-95% tumor control. Preferred for poor surgical candidates; small risk of malignant transformation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SRS, Gamma Knife}
Superior Canal Resurfacing/Plugging [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Surgical repair of symptomatic SSCD via a middle cranial fossa (or transmastoid) approach, plugging and/or resurfacing the dehiscent canal to eliminate the third-window effect.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka canal plugging}
Translabyrinthine Approach [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Sacrifices hearing to remove vestibular schwannoma via the labyrinth; offers early identification of the facial nerve at the fundus and wide access regardless of tumor size. Used when hearing is non-serviceable.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka translab}
Tympanoplasty [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · vetted] — Surgical repair of TM perforation ± ossicular chain reconstruction. Grafts: temporalis fascia, tragal perichondrium, cartilage. Underlay vs overlay technique.  (Cummings 7e)
Vestibular Evoked Myogenic Potential [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Sound-evoked reflex recordings: cervical VEMP (saccule/inferior vestibular nerve, SCM) and ocular VEMP (utricle/superior vestibular nerve). In SSCD, cVEMP thresholds are abnormally low and oVEMP amplitudes abnormally high.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5408023/)  {aka VEMP, cVEMP, oVEMP}
Video Head Impulse Test [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Bedside/video quantification of high-frequency VOR gain for each semicircular canal; a corrective catch-up saccade with reduced gain indicates canal hypofunction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka vHIT}
Videonystagmography & Caloric Testing [procedure · Otology/Neurotology · draft] — Records eye movements to quantify spontaneous/positional nystagmus and, via bithermal caloric irrigation, unilateral vestibular (horizontal canal) weakness. Calorics probe low-frequency VOR function.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka VNG, ENG}
Labyrinthine Artery [vessel · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — Usually branch of AICA (85%). End artery — occlusion causes sudden SNHL and vestibular loss. Travels through IAC.  (Cummings 7e)
Sigmoid Sinus [vessel · Otology/Neurotology · temporal-bone · paired · vetted] — S-shaped venous sinus in posterior fossa. Lateral boundary of mastoid surgery. Thrombosis is complication of coalescent mastoiditis.  (Cummings 7e)

--- RHINOLOGY (110) ---
Agger Nasi Cell [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Most anterior ethmoid air cell anterior to the middle turbinate attachment; a key landmark for the frontal recess and frontal sinus drainage.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Anterior Skull Base [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Floor of the anterior cranial fossa formed by the frontal bone, cribriform plate/ethmoid roof, and planum sphenoidale; surgical corridor for endoscopic resection of sinonasal and skull base tumors.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ASB}
Basal Lamella [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Coronal bony attachment of the middle turbinate to the lamina papyracea; the surgical boundary separating anterior from posterior ethmoid cells.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Basiocciput [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Basilar part of the occipital bone forming the lower clivus and fusing with the sphenoid.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Body of Sphenoid [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Central cube containing the sphenoid sinus and supporting the sella turcica.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cavernous Sinus [anatomy · Rhinology · space · skull-base · paired · draft] — Paired dural venous sinus lateral to the sphenoid sinus and sella. Within its lumen run the internal carotid artery and abducens nerve (CN VI — the only cranial nerve that travels free within the sinus, adjacent to the ICA); its lateral wall carries CN III, IV, V1 and V2 in a superior-to-inferior order. Cavernous sinus thrombosis is a dreaded complication of sphenoid/facial infection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Clivus [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Sloping midline surface formed by the fused sphenoid body and basiocciput, posterior to the dorsum sellae.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cribriform Plate [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Perforated horizontal ethmoid plate transmitting olfactory fibers; the thin lateral lamella is the most common site of iatrogenic CSF leak. Keros classification grades its depth (I–III).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Crista Ethmoidalis [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Small bony crest of the perpendicular process of the palatine bone, located just anterior to the sphenopalatine foramen. It is the constant surgical landmark for identifying and ligating/cauterizing the sphenopalatine artery as it exits onto the lateral nasal wall.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ethmoidal crest}
Crista Galli [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Midline ethmoid ridge projecting superiorly for attachment of the falx cerebri.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Dorsum Sellae [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Posterior wall of the sella turcica, continuous inferiorly with the clivus.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ethmoid Bone [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Central bone contributing the cribriform plate, perpendicular plate, lamina papyracea and ethmoid air cells with the middle and superior turbinates.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ethmoid Bone [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Midline bone forming part of the anterior cranial base, nasal roof and medial orbital walls.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ethmoid Bulla [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Largest, most constant anterior ethmoid air cell bulging into the middle meatus; forms the posterior boundary of the hiatus semilunaris and ostiomeatal complex.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ethmoid Labyrinth [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Paired lateral masses containing the ethmoid air cells, bounded superiorly by the fovea ethmoidalis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ethmoid Sinus [anatomy · Rhinology · space · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Labyrinth of anterior and posterior air cells; the anterior wall of the sphenoid and lamina papyracea border it. Most common origin of orbital complications in children.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ethmoid air cells}
Foramen Cecum [anatomy · Rhinology · foramen · skull-base · midline · draft] — Small midline pit between the frontal bone and crista galli; may transmit an emissary vein.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Foramen Lacerum [anatomy · Rhinology · foramen · skull-base · paired · draft] — Cartilage-filled gap at the petrous apex–sphenoid junction; the internal carotid artery crosses its superior surface (does not truly pass through it) as it transitions from the petrous to the cavernous segment. The vidian canal opens onto its anterior margin, making it an ICA landmark in expanded endonasal approaches.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Fovea Ethmoidalis [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Ethmoid roof formed by the frontal bone, lying lateral and superior to the cribriform plate. The anterior ethmoidal artery crosses it and is a key surgical danger zone.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ethmoid roof}
Frontal Sinus [anatomy · Rhinology · space · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Drains via the frontal recess (an inverted funnel), not a true duct. Posterior table erosion allows intracranial spread; obstruction can cause Pott puffy tumor.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Greater Wing of Sphenoid [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Forms the anterior middle cranial fossa floor; transmits foramina rotundum, ovale and spinosum.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Inferior Turbinate [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Largest turbinate; the nasolacrimal duct drains beneath it at the inferior meatus. Hypertrophy is a common cause of nasal obstruction treated with turbinate reduction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka IT}
Jugular Tubercle [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Occipital elevation superomedial to the hypoglossal canal and jugular foramen.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Lamina Papyracea [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Paper-thin medial orbital wall separating the ethmoid sinus from the orbit; its dehiscence or breach is the route of ethmoid orbital complications and inadvertent orbital entry in FESS.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka os planum}
Lesser Wing of Sphenoid [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Forms the posterior anterior-cranial-fossa margin and the anterior clinoid process; its root bounds the optic canal.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Maxillary Sinus [anatomy · Rhinology · space · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Largest paranasal sinus; its natural ostium lies high on the medial wall, draining superiorly into the middle meatus, which predisposes to poor gravity-dependent drainage.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka antrum of Highmore}
Middle Turbinate [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Attaches to skull base at the vertical basal lamella; a key surgical landmark whose axilla marks the frontal recess. Concha bullosa is pneumatization of the middle turbinate.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka MT}
Nasolacrimal Duct [anatomy · Rhinology · space · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Drains tears from the lacrimal sac into the inferior meatus via the valve of Hasner; obstruction causes epiphora and dacryocystitis, treated by dacryocystorhinostomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka NLD}
Occipital Bone [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Forms the posterior cranial fossa and encircles the foramen magnum.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Occipital Condyle [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Paired condyles flanking the foramen magnum that articulate with the atlas.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Onodi Cell [anatomy · Rhinology · space · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — A posterior ethmoid cell that pneumatizes superolaterally over/above the sphenoid sinus. Pearl: when present, the optic nerve and internal carotid artery are intimately related to (and may dehisce into) the Onodi cell rather than the sphenoid — unrecognized, it is a classic cause of optic-nerve injury during posterior ethmoidectomy. Always identify it on preoperative CT.  (Stammberger Functional Endoscopic Sinus Surgery)  {aka sphenoethmoidal cell}
Orbital Apex [anatomy · Rhinology · space · skull-base · paired · draft] — Convergence of the optic canal and superior orbital fissure at the posterior orbit, with the annulus of Zinn as the common origin of the rectus muscles. Orbital apex syndrome = superior orbital fissure syndrome (CN III, IV, V1, VI) plus optic-nerve (CN II) involvement; can complicate invasive fungal sinusitis or posterior ethmoid/sphenoid disease.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Ostiomeatal Complex [anatomy · Rhinology · space · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Common final drainage pathway of the maxillary, anterior ethmoid, and frontal sinuses into the middle meatus. Obstruction here is the central mechanism of chronic rhinosinusitis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka OMC, osteomeatal complex}
Parietal Bone [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Paired calvarial bones contributing to the lateral skull vault.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Perpendicular Plate of Ethmoid [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Descending midline ethmoid plate forming the superior bony nasal septum, continuous with the septal cartilage and vomer.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Perpendicular Plate of Ethmoid [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Forms the superior bony nasal septum.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Petro-occipital Fissure [anatomy · Rhinology · joint · skull-base · paired · draft] — Fissure between the petrous temporal bone and the basiocciput, ending at the jugular foramen.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Planum Sphenoidale [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Flat surface of the sphenoid between the ethmoid and the tuberculum sellae.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Pterygoid Process [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · paired · draft] — Descends from the sphenoid body/greater wing junction; contains medial and lateral pterygoid plates.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Pterygopalatine Fossa [anatomy · Rhinology · space · skull-base · paired · draft] — Fat-filled surgical crossroads between the maxillary sinus (anterior), pterygoid plates (posterior) and palatine bone (medial). Contents: V2, the pterygopalatine (sphenopalatine) ganglion and the terminal third of the internal maxillary artery. Communicates with the middle cranial fossa via foramen rotundum, the nasal cavity via the sphenopalatine foramen, the orbit via the inferior orbital fissure, the pterygoid canal (vidian) and the greater/lesser palatine canals — a common tumor spread route (JNA, ACC perineural).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka PPF}
Sella Turcica [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Saddle-shaped sphenoid depression housing the pituitary gland; its floor is the roof of the sphenoid sinus and the endoscopic corridor to the gland.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka sella}
Septal (Quadrangular) Cartilage [anatomy · Rhinology · cartilage · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Quadrangular cartilage forming the anterior nasal septum; continuous posterosuperiorly with the ethmoid perpendicular plate and posteroinferiorly with the vomer.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Sesamoid Cartilage [anatomy · Rhinology · cartilage · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Small accessory cartilages within the fibrofatty tissue lateral to the lower lateral cartilage along the alar margin.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Spheno-occipital Synchondrosis [anatomy · Rhinology · joint · skull-base · midline · draft] — Cartilaginous junction between the sphenoid body and basiocciput; fuses in late adolescence and drives clival growth.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Sphenoethmoidal Junction [anatomy · Rhinology · joint · skull-base · midline · draft] — Articulation between the sphenoid and the posterior ethmoid.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Sphenofrontal Suture [anatomy · Rhinology · joint · skull-base · paired · draft] — Suture between the lesser/greater wing of the sphenoid and the frontal bone.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Sphenoid Bone [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Central keystone of the cranial base; articulates with all other cranial bones. Forms much of the middle cranial fossa.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Sphenoid Sinus [anatomy · Rhinology · space · skull-base · midline · draft] — Deepest sinus; its roof forms the sellar floor and its lateral walls relate to the optic nerve and internal carotid artery. Gateway for endoscopic transsphenoidal pituitary surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Sphenopalatine Foramen [anatomy · Rhinology · foramen · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Opening at the posterior end of the superior meatus transmitting the sphenopalatine artery and nasopalatine nerve; crista ethmoidalis is its anterior bony landmark for SPA ligation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Sphenopetrosal Fissure [anatomy · Rhinology · joint · skull-base · paired · draft] — Fissure between the greater wing of the sphenoid and the petrous temporal bone.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Squamous Occipital [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Expanded plate posterior to the foramen magnum forming the posterior fossa wall.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Superior Turbinate [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Smallest ethmoid turbinate; the sphenoethmoidal recess and posterior ethmoid cells drain in relation to it.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Tuberculum Sellae [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · skull-base · midline · draft] — Bony ridge marking the anterior boundary of the sella turcica.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Uncinate Process [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Sickle-shaped bone whose removal (uncinectomy) is the first step of FESS, opening the ethmoid infundibulum and maxillary sinus. Superior attachment determines frontal recess drainage.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Upper Lateral Cartilage [anatomy · Rhinology · cartilage · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Paired middle-vault cartilages fused to the dorsal septum; their caudal scroll overlaps the lower lateral cartilage and the internal nasal valve angle.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vidian Canal [anatomy · Rhinology · foramen · skull-base · paired · draft] — Bony canal through the sphenoid base transmitting the vidian nerve and artery; runs from the anterior aspect of foramen lacerum to the pterygopalatine fossa. Endoscopically it sits at the floor of the sphenoid sinus and points to the petrous ICA — a key skull-base drilling landmark.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka pterygoid canal}
Vomer [anatomy · Rhinology · bone · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Midline bone forming the posteroinferior bony nasal septum; articulates with the ethmoid perpendicular plate, sphenoid, maxilla and palatine crests.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Bent-Kuhn Criteria [concept · Rhinology · draft] — Five major diagnostic criteria for AFRS: nasal polyposis, fungi on staining, eosinophilic mucin without invasion, type I hypersensitivity to fungi, and characteristic CT findings (heterogeneous hyperattenuation).  (https://www.otoscape.com/eponyms/bent-kuhn-criteria-for-afrs.html; https://www.backtable.com/shows/ent/articles/allergic-fungal-rhinosinusitis-symptoms-diagnostic-criteria)  {aka Bent and Kuhn criteria}
Beta-2 Transferrin [concept · Rhinology · draft] — Highly specific CSF marker (also beta-trace protein) used to confirm CSF rhinorrhea from clear nasal drainage before localization and repair.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka β2-transferrin, tau transferrin}
Dulguerov TNM Staging [concept · Rhinology · draft] — TNM system for esthesioneuroblastoma refining T stage by extent of skull base, orbit, and intracranial/dural involvement; considered more prognostic than Kadish for advanced tumors.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Dulguerov classification}
Kadish Staging [concept · Rhinology · draft] — Clinical staging of esthesioneuroblastoma: A confined to the nasal cavity, B extends to paranasal sinuses, C beyond sinuses (Morita added D for nodal/distant metastasis).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Kadish-Morita}
Keros Classification [concept · Rhinology · skull-base · draft] — Grades the depth of the olfactory fossa by the vertical height of the lateral lamella of the cribriform plate: Keros I 1–3 mm, II 4–7 mm, III 8–16 mm. A deeper fossa (Keros III) and a thin lateral lamella carry the highest risk of iatrogenic CSF leak/skull-base injury during ethmoidectomy; asymmetry between sides is an added danger sign.  (Stammberger Functional Endoscopic Sinus Surgery)  {aka Keros grade}
Keystone Area [concept · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Bony-cartilaginous junction where the caudal nasal bones overlap the cephalic upper lateral cartilages and dorsal septum; critical dorsal support in rhinoplasty.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Lund-Mackay Score [concept · Rhinology · draft] — CT staging of rhinosinusitis scoring each sinus group 0–2 and the OMC 0 or 2, per side, for a maximum of 24; standard radiologic severity measure used before FESS.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Lund-Mackay, LM score}
Dupilumab [drug · Rhinology · draft] — Anti-IL-4Rα monoclonal antibody blocking IL-4 and IL-13 signaling; first biologic FDA-approved (2019) for CRSwNP. Reduces polyp size, congestion, and improves smell in type 2 disease.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12535206/; https://bulletin.entnet.org/clinical-patient-care/article/22881642/biologics-for-chronic-rhinosinusitis-with-nasal-polyps)  {aka Dupixent}
Intranasal Corticosteroids [drug · Rhinology · draft] — First-line anti-inflammatory therapy for CRS, nasal polyps, and allergic rhinitis; reduce polyp size and congestion with minimal systemic absorption. Epistaxis is the most common local side effect.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka INCS, nasal steroid spray}
Mepolizumab [drug · Rhinology · draft] — Anti-IL-5 monoclonal antibody FDA-approved (2021) for CRSwNP; depletes eosinophils to shrink polyps. Tezepelumab (anti-TSLP) is a newer fourth biologic approved for CRSwNP.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12535206/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35510314/)  {aka Nucala}
Omalizumab [drug · Rhinology · draft] — Anti-IgE monoclonal antibody FDA-approved (2020) for CRSwNP; binds free IgE to reduce type 2 inflammation and polyp burden, particularly in allergic/asthmatic patients.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12535206/; https://bulletin.entnet.org/clinical-patient-care/article/22881642/biologics-for-chronic-rhinosinusitis-with-nasal-polyps)  {aka Xolair}
Abducens Nerve (CN VI) [nerve · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Motor to lateral rectus. Longest intracranial course makes it vulnerable to raised ICP; uniquely runs THROUGH the cavernous sinus adjacent to the ICA (not in the wall), so it is the first nerve affected in cavernous sinus/petrous apex (Gradenigo) pathology. Palsy gives horizontal diplopia.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka CN VI, sixth cranial nerve}
Greater Palatine Nerve [nerve · Rhinology · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — V2 branch descending through the greater palatine canal to supply the hard palate and postganglionic parasympathetic fibres from the pterygopalatine ganglion. The greater palatine foramen (near the second molar) is the landmark for a maxillary nerve block.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka anterior palatine nerve}
Lesser Palatine Nerve [nerve · Rhinology · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — V2 branch supplying the soft palate and tonsillar region via the lesser palatine foramen; carries taste from the soft palate back to the geniculate ganglion via the greater petrosal nerve.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)
Oculomotor Nerve (CN III) [nerve · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Motor to all extraocular muscles except superior oblique and lateral rectus, plus levator palpebrae and parasympathetic pupillary constriction. Traverses the cavernous sinus and superior orbital fissure; palsy gives ptosis and a 'down-and-out' eye.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka CN III, third cranial nerve}
Ophthalmic Nerve (V1) [nerve · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Purely sensory first division of CN V. Enters the orbit via the superior orbital fissure; gives frontal, lacrimal and nasociliary branches. Anterior ethmoidal branch of nasociliary is the afferent limb of the sneeze reflex and supplies the anterior skull base.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Netter Atlas of Human Anatomy)  {aka V1, first trigeminal division}
Pterygopalatine Ganglion [nerve · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Largest parasympathetic ganglion, suspended from V2 in the pterygopalatine fossa. Parasympathetic root is the greater petrosal (from CN VII); relays secretomotor fibres to the lacrimal gland and nasal/palatine mucosa. Target for sphenopalatine block in epistaxis and cluster headache.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka sphenopalatine ganglion, Meckel's ganglion}
Sphenopalatine (Pterygopalatine) Ganglion [nerve · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Largest parasympathetic ganglion of the head, suspended from V2 in the pterygopalatine fossa. Parasympathetic secretomotor fibers arrive via the greater superficial petrosal nerve (as part of the vidian nerve) and synapse here to supply the lacrimal gland and nasal/palatine mucosal glands; sympathetic fibers pass through without synapsing.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka pterygopalatine ganglion, SPG}
Trochlear Nerve (CN IV) [nerve · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Thinnest cranial nerve; only one exiting dorsally and the sole crossed nerve. Motor to superior oblique. Runs in the lateral cavernous sinus wall to the superior orbital fissure; palsy causes vertical diplopia and head tilt.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka CN IV, fourth cranial nerve}
Vidian Nerve [nerve · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Nerve of the pterygoid canal, formed by the greater superficial petrosal (parasympathetic) plus the deep petrosal (sympathetic) nerves. Runs anteriorly through the vidian canal to reach the sphenopalatine ganglion. Endoscopic vidian neurectomy is used for refractory vasomotor rhinitis; the canal is a reliable landmark to the second genu of the petrous internal carotid artery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka nerve of pterygoid canal}
Acute Bacterial Rhinosinusitis [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Suspected when viral URI symptoms persist >10 days, worsen after initial improvement (double-worsening), or are severe (fever, purulence, facial pain). Top pathogens: S. pneumoniae, H. influenzae, M. catarrhalis.  (AAO-HNSF Adult Sinusitis Clinical Practice Guideline)  {aka ABRS, acute bacterial sinusitis}
Allergic Fungal Rhinosinusitis [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Type I hypersensitivity to fungi producing eosinophilic 'allergic mucin' without tissue invasion, often unilateral with bony expansion in atopic young patients. Treated with surgical debridement plus topical/oral steroids; dupilumab is under study.  (https://www.annallergy.org/article/S1081-1206(23)00126-6/fulltext; https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04684524)  {aka AFRS, AFS}
Allergic Rhinitis [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — IgE-mediated type I hypersensitivity of the nasal mucosa causing sneezing, rhinorrhea, congestion, and itch. Diagnosed by history plus skin-prick or serum specific-IgE testing; classified seasonal vs perennial.  (AAO-HNSF Clinical Practice Guideline: Allergic Rhinitis)  {aka AR, hay fever}
Cavernous Sinus Thrombosis [pathology · Rhinology · skull-base · draft] — Septic thrombosis (Chandler V) spreading from sphenoid/ethmoid sinuses; bilateral eye findings, cranial neuropathies (III, IV, V1-2, VI), and sepsis. Requires antibiotics and often anticoagulation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CST}
Chronic Rhinosinusitis [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — ≥12 weeks of sinonasal inflammation with objective evidence (endoscopy or CT) plus ≥2 cardinal symptoms. Endotyped as type 2 (eosinophilic) vs non-type 2; phenotyped with (CRSwNP) or without (CRSsNP) polyps.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum; EPOS 2020)  {aka CRS}
CSF Rhinorrhea [pathology · Rhinology · skull-base · draft] — Leakage of cerebrospinal fluid into the nose from a skull base defect (traumatic, iatrogenic, or spontaneous with elevated ICP). Beta-2 transferrin confirms; high-resolution CT and MRI/cisternography localize the defect.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CSF leak}
Encephalocele / Meningoencephalocele [pathology · Rhinology · skull-base · draft] — Herniation of meninges (± brain) through a skull base defect, often at the cribriform/lateral lamella or lateral sphenoid recess; presents as a nasal mass or CSF leak. Repaired endoscopically with multilayer reconstruction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka meningoencephalocele}
Epistaxis [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Anterior bleeds (90%) arise from Kiesselbach plexus and respond to cautery/packing; posterior bleeds from the sphenopalatine/Woodruff region may need posterior packing, SPA ligation, or embolization.  (AAO-HNSF Clinical Practice Guideline: Nosebleed (Epistaxis))  {aka nosebleed}
Esthesioneuroblastoma [pathology · Rhinology · skull-base · draft] — Olfactory neuroblastoma arising from cribriform olfactory neuroepithelium; presents with nasal obstruction/epistaxis and dumbbell tumor crossing the skull base. Hyams grade histologically; treated with craniofacial/endoscopic resection plus radiation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka olfactory neuroblastoma, ENB}
Hereditary Hemorrhagic Telangiectasia [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Autosomal dominant (ENG, ACVRL1) vascular dysplasia; Curaçao criteria (epistaxis, telangiectasias, visceral AVMs, family history). Recurrent epistaxis is the commonest feature; bevacizumab and laser/KTP help.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka HHT, Osler-Weber-Rendu}
Inverted Papilloma [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Benign but locally aggressive Schneiderian papilloma, usually unilateral from the lateral nasal wall/maxillary sinus with a focal osteitic attachment (CT tumor origin); ~10% harbor SCC. Krouse staging; treated by endoscopic resection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Schneiderian papilloma, IP}
Juvenile Nasopharyngeal Angiofibroma [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Benign highly vascular tumor of adolescent males arising near the sphenopalatine foramen; presents with obstruction and epistaxis. Anterior bowing of the posterior maxillary wall (Holman-Miller sign); preop embolization then resection. Biopsy contraindicated.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka JNA, angiofibroma}
Nasal Polyposis (CRSwNP) [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Bilateral edematous polyps arising from the ethmoids/middle meatus, typically eosinophilic type 2 inflammation. Associated with asthma and aspirin sensitivity (Samter's triad / AERD). Unilateral polyp warrants tumor workup.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum; EPOS 2020)  {aka CRSwNP, nasal polyps}
Pituitary Adenoma [pathology · Rhinology · skull-base · midline · draft] — Benign sellar tumor; macroadenomas compress the optic chiasm causing bitemporal hemianopsia. Functioning types secrete prolactin (prolactinomas—medical first), GH, or ACTH. Removed via endoscopic endonasal transsphenoidal approach.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka pituitary tumor}
Pott Puffy Tumor [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Frontal osteomyelitis with a subperiosteal forehead abscess complicating frontal sinusitis; associated with epidural/intracranial abscess and requires imaging, drainage, and prolonged IV antibiotics.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Pott's puffy tumor}
Retrobulbar Hematoma [pathology · Rhinology · draft] — Orbital compartment syndrome from bleeding (classically a retracted, transected anterior/posterior ethmoidal artery) during endoscopic sinus surgery. Presents with proptosis, tense orbit, decreasing vision and a relative afferent pupillary defect; a surgical emergency requiring urgent lateral canthotomy and inferior cantholysis to prevent permanent blindness.  (Stammberger Functional Endoscopic Sinus Surgery)  {aka retro-orbital hematoma, orbital hematoma}
Sinonasal Squamous Cell Carcinoma [pathology · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Most common sinonasal malignancy, frequently maxillary sinus; risk factors include smoking and occupational exposures. Often advanced at diagnosis; managed with resection plus adjuvant chemoradiation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka sinonasal carcinoma}
Allergen Immunotherapy [procedure · Rhinology · draft] — Subcutaneous (SCIT) or sublingual (SLIT) desensitization inducing regulatory T cells and blocking IgG4; the only disease-modifying therapy for allergic rhinitis, offered when pharmacotherapy fails.  (AAO-HNSF Clinical Practice Guideline: Allergic Rhinitis)  {aka SCIT, SLIT, immunotherapy}
Dacryocystorhinostomy [procedure · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Endoscopic or external creation of a fistula between the lacrimal sac and nasal cavity anterior to the middle turbinate for nasolacrimal duct obstruction causing epiphora/dacryocystitis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka DCR}
Endoscopic Endonasal Transsphenoidal Approach [procedure · Rhinology · skull-base · midline · draft] — Minimally invasive corridor through the nostril and sphenoid sinus to the sella for pituitary and parasellar lesions, often a combined ENT-neurosurgery approach with nasoseptal flap reconstruction of the defect.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka EEA, transsphenoidal surgery}
Endoscopic Skull Base Repair [procedure · Rhinology · skull-base · draft] — Multilayer endoscopic closure of skull base defects using free grafts (fascia, fat) and/or vascularized nasoseptal flap; lumbar drainage and treatment of raised ICP improve success in spontaneous leaks.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CSF leak repair}
Endoscopic Sphenopalatine Artery Ligation [procedure · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Endoscopic clipping/cautery of the SPA at the sphenopalatine foramen behind the middle turbinate tail; definitive treatment for refractory posterior epistaxis with high success and low morbidity.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SPA ligation, ESPAL}
Functional Endoscopic Sinus Surgery [procedure · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Endoscopic mucosa-sparing surgery restoring sinus ventilation and drainage (uncinectomy, maxillary antrostomy, ethmoidectomy, sphenoidotomy, frontal recess dissection) for CRS refractory to medical therapy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka FESS, ESS, endoscopic sinus surgery}
Nasal Packing [procedure · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · draft] — Anterior (absorbable or expandable) and posterior (balloon/gauze) packing tamponades epistaxis; posterior packing risks hypoxia and requires monitoring for the nasopulmonary reflex.  (AAO-HNSF Clinical Practice Guideline: Nosebleed (Epistaxis))
Nasoseptal Flap [procedure · Rhinology · skull-base · draft] — Pedicled Hadad-Bassagasteguy vascularized mucoperichondrial flap based on the posterior septal branch of the sphenopalatine artery; workhorse reconstruction for anterior skull base and sellar defects to prevent CSF leak.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Hadad flap, HBF}
Anterior Ethmoidal Artery [vessel · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Branch of the ophthalmic (internal carotid) artery crossing the ethmoid roof; retraction into the orbit after transection can cause a sight-threatening orbital hematoma.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka AEA}
Internal Maxillary Artery [vessel · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Larger terminal branch of the external carotid; its third (pterygopalatine) part enters the fossa and divides into the sphenopalatine, descending palatine, infraorbital and posterior superior alveolar arteries. Proximal control here (or transantral ligation) is a classic maneuver for intractable posterior epistaxis and the feeding supply of a juvenile nasopharyngeal angiofibroma.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka maxillary artery, IMAX}
Kiesselbach Plexus [vessel · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Anterior septal anastomosis (Little's area) of the sphenopalatine, anterior ethmoidal, greater palatine, and superior labial arteries; site of the majority of anterior epistaxis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Little's area, Kiesselbach's plexus}
Ophthalmic Artery [vessel · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — First intradural branch of the internal carotid; enters the orbit through the optic canal (inferolateral to the nerve) and gives the anterior and posterior ethmoidal arteries that supply the anterior skull base. This ICA–ECA anastomosis is why external carotid embolization for epistaxis carries a risk of retrograde central retinal artery occlusion.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Posterior Ethmoidal Artery [vessel · Rhinology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Branch of the ophthalmic artery crossing the skull base ~2–3 mm anterior to the optic canal, roughly 12 mm posterior to the anterior ethmoidal artery. Injury during posterior ethmoidectomy risks retraction into the orbit and retrobulbar hematoma; it lies closer to the optic nerve than the AEA, so posterior dissection here demands caution.  (Stammberger Functional Endoscopic Sinus Surgery)  {aka PEA}
Posterior Septal Artery [vessel · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Terminal septal branch of the sphenopalatine artery that crosses the anterior face of the sphenoid (between the sinus ostium and the choana) to supply the posterior nasal septum. It is the vascular pedicle of the Hadad-Bassagasteguy nasoseptal flap — the workhorse reconstruction for endoscopic skull-base defects; its pedicle must be preserved and the flap raised before sphenoidotomy in planned skull-base cases.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka posterior septal branch}
Sphenopalatine Artery [vessel · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Terminal branch of the maxillary artery entering the nose through the sphenopalatine foramen behind the middle turbinate tail; principal source of severe posterior epistaxis and target of endoscopic ligation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SPA}
Woodruff Plexus [vessel · Rhinology · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Venous plexus of the posterior inferior meatus/nasopharynx; classic source of posterior epistaxis in older hypertensive patients that is difficult to control with anterior packing.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Woodruff's plexus}

--- LARYNGOLOGY (78) ---
Apex of Arytenoid [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Superior tip of the arytenoid cartilage; articulates with the corniculate cartilage and lies within the aryepiglottic fold.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Aryepiglottic Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — Oblique interarytenoid fibers continuing into the aryepiglottic fold; acts as a sphincter narrowing the laryngeal inlet during swallowing.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Arytenoid Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Paired pyramidal cartilages atop the cricoid; the vocal process anchors the vocal ligament and the muscular process receives the posterior and lateral cricoarytenoid muscles. Rotation/gliding at the cricoarytenoid joint abducts and adducts the vocal folds.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Conus Elasticus (Cricothyroid Membrane) [anatomy · Laryngology · membrane · larynx · midline · draft] — Elastic membrane rising from the cricoid arch to end superiorly as the free vocal ligaments; its anterior thickening is the cricothyroid membrane traversed in cricothyrotomy. Bounds the subglottis medially.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka cricovocal membrane}
Corniculate Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Small paired elastic cartilage (of Santorini) sitting on the arytenoid apex within the aryepiglottic fold.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka cartilage of Santorini}
Cricoarytenoid Joint [anatomy · Laryngology · joint · larynx · paired · draft] — Synovial joint between the arytenoid base and the cricoid lamina permitting the rocking/gliding that opens and closes the glottis. Fixation (e.g., rheumatoid arthritis, intubation trauma) mimics vocal fold paralysis and is distinguished by palpation under anesthesia and by EMG.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cricoid Arch [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · midline · draft] — Narrow anterior portion of the signet-ring cricoid cartilage; gives origin to the cricothyroid and lateral cricoarytenoid muscles.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cricoid Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · midline · draft] — Only complete cartilaginous ring of the airway (signet-ring shape, tall posterior lamina). Forms the subglottis; narrowest point of the pediatric airway and the level implicated in subglottic stenosis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cricoid Lamina [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · midline · draft] — Broad posterior plate of the cricoid; bears the cricoarytenoid facets superiorly and gives origin to the posterior cricoarytenoid muscle.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cricopharyngeus Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · midline · draft] — Horizontal fibers of the inferior pharyngeal constrictor forming the upper esophageal sphincter. Failure to relax (cricopharyngeal dysfunction) raises hypopharyngeal pressure and predisposes to Zenker diverticulum through Killian's dehiscence.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka upper esophageal sphincter}
Cricothyroid Joint [anatomy · Laryngology · joint · larynx · paired · draft] — Synovial articulation between the inferior thyroid cornu and the cricoid; visor-like rotation by the cricothyroid muscle lengthens and tenses the vocal folds to raise pitch.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cricothyroid Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — Tensor of the vocal folds (raises pitch); the only intrinsic laryngeal muscle NOT innervated by the recurrent laryngeal nerve—it is supplied by the external branch of the superior laryngeal nerve.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CT muscle}
Cricotracheal Ligament [anatomy · Laryngology · ligament · larynx · midline · draft] — Connects the lower cricoid border to the first tracheal ring; divided and reapproximated in cricotracheal resection for subglottic stenosis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cuneiform Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Small paired elastic cartilage (of Wrisberg) within the aryepiglottic fold, anterolateral to the corniculate, providing support.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka cartilage of Wrisberg}
Epiglottis [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · midline · draft] — Leaf-shaped elastic cartilage that deflects the bolus during swallowing. Its petiole attaches to the thyroid cartilage; the pre-epiglottic space is a route of supraglottic cancer spread.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Hyoepiglottic Ligament [anatomy · Laryngology · ligament · larynx · midline · draft] — Connects the anterior epiglottis to the hyoid body, forming the floor of the pre-epiglottic (Boyer) space; tenting it opens the vallecula during suspension laryngoscopy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Inferior Cornu of Thyroid Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Lower horn of the thyroid lamina; its medial facet articulates with the cricoid to form the cricothyroid joint.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka inferior thyroid horn}
Interarytenoid Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · midline · draft] — Transverse and oblique fibers bridging the arytenoids to close the posterior (cartilaginous) glottis. The only intrinsic laryngeal muscle with bilateral RLN innervation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka arytenoid muscle}
Laryngeal Prominence [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · midline · draft] — Anterior midline fusion of the thyroid laminae ('Adam's apple'); anterior commissure lies on its inner surface.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Adam's apple}
Lateral Cricoarytenoid Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — Intrinsic adductor that rotates the arytenoid to close the membranous glottis for phonation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka LCA}
Left Thyroid Lamina [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Left quadrilateral plate of the thyroid cartilage; fuses anteriorly with the right lamina at the laryngeal prominence.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Muscular Process of Arytenoid [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Lateral projection of the arytenoid; insertion of the posterior and lateral cricoarytenoid muscles that rotate the arytenoid.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Oblique Line of Thyroid Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Ridge on the outer thyroid lamina giving attachment to the sternothyroid, thyrohyoid, and inferior pharyngeal constrictor muscles.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Petiole of Epiglottis [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · midline · draft] — Narrow inferior stalk of the epiglottis attached to the inner thyroid cartilage by the thyroepiglottic ligament.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Posterior Cricoarytenoid Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — The ONLY abductor of the vocal folds, opening the glottis for respiration. Bilateral loss produces adducted folds and airway compromise.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka PCA}
Quadrangular Membrane [anatomy · Laryngology · membrane · larynx · paired · draft] — Upper fibroelastic membrane of the endolarynx; its free lower edge forms the vestibular (false vocal) ligament and its upper edge the aryepiglottic fold.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Reinke's Space [anatomy · Laryngology · space · larynx · paired · draft] — The superficial layer of the lamina propria, a loose gelatinous potential space essential to the mucosal wave. Fluid accumulation here (polypoid corditis/Reinke edema) is classically caused by smoking.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka superficial lamina propria}
Right Thyroid Lamina [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Right quadrilateral plate of the thyroid cartilage; fuses anteriorly with the left lamina at the laryngeal prominence.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Superior Cornu of Thyroid Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Upper horn of the thyroid lamina; connected to the greater cornu of the hyoid by the lateral thyrohyoid ligament.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka superior thyroid horn}
Thyroarytenoid Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — Intrinsic adductor forming the body of the vocal fold; its medial vocalis fibers fine-tune tension. Primary target for botulinum toxin in adductor spasmodic dysphonia.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka vocalis}
