Thyroepiglottic Ligament [anatomy · Laryngology · ligament · larynx · midline · draft] — Tethers the epiglottic petiole to the inner thyroid lamina just above the anterior commissure; a route of anterior tumor spread into the pre-epiglottic space.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Thyroepiglottic Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — Superolateral thyroarytenoid fibers running to the epiglottis; widens the laryngeal inlet.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Thyrohyoid Membrane [anatomy · Laryngology · membrane · larynx · midline · draft] — Fibroelastic sheet suspending the thyroid cartilage from the hyoid; pierced by the internal branch of the superior laryngeal nerve and superior laryngeal vessels.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Thyroid Cartilage [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · midline · draft] — Largest laryngeal cartilage; two laminae meet anteriorly at the laryngeal prominence. Its inner surface at the anterior commissure is the attachment (Broyles ligament) for the vocal ligaments and a route of tumor spread.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Adam's apple}
Vestibular Ligament (False Cord) [anatomy · Laryngology · ligament · larynx · paired · draft] — Thickened inferior free margin of the quadrangular membrane forming the framework of the false vocal fold above the laryngeal ventricle.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vocal Fold Lamina Propria (Layered) [anatomy · Laryngology · membrane · larynx · paired · draft] — Trilaminar cover of the vocal fold: superficial (Reinke space, gelatinous), intermediate (elastic), and deep (collagenous) layers; the intermediate + deep layers form the vocal ligament. The pliable superficial layer generates the mucosal wave; scar or lesions that stiffen it cause dysphonia (Hirano body-cover model).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka body-cover model}
Vocal Ligament [anatomy · Laryngology · ligament · larynx · paired · draft] — The intermediate and deep layers of the lamina propria; the free upper edge of the conus elasticus running from the vocal process of the arytenoid to the anterior commissure. Forms the medial edge of the vocal fold.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vocal Process of Arytenoid [anatomy · Laryngology · cartilage · larynx · paired · draft] — Anterior projection of the arytenoid that anchors the posterior end of the vocal ligament; a key landmark for medialization and injection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vocalis Muscle [anatomy · Laryngology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — Medial deep fibers of the thyroarytenoid lying against the vocal ligament; fine-tunes vocal fold tension and pitch.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cotton-Myer Classification [concept · Laryngology · draft] — Grades subglottic stenosis by luminal obstruction: Grade I 0-50%, II 51-70%, III 71-99%, IV no detectable lumen. Assessed under anesthesia by comparing the largest endotracheal tube passed (with an air leak) against age-expected size.  (https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/000348949410300410; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK563265/)  {aka Myer-Cotton grading}
Killian's Dehiscence [concept · Laryngology · draft] — Triangular area of weakness in the posterior hypopharyngeal wall between the oblique thyropharyngeus and horizontal cricopharyngeus fibers. This is the site through which a Zenker (pulsion) diverticulum herniates.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Killian triangle}
Non-Recurrent Laryngeal Nerve [concept · Laryngology · draft] — Rare variant (~0.5-1%) almost always on the RIGHT, associated with an aberrant right subclavian artery (arteria lusoria). The nerve branches directly off the cervical vagus and passes medially to the larynx without looping under the subclavian — an important cause of unexpected intraoperative RLN injury during thyroidectomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Bailey's Head and Neck Surgery)  {aka non-recurrent nerve}
Reflux Finding Score (RFS) [concept · Laryngology · draft] — Eight-item laryngoscopic grading of reflux signs (subglottic edema, ventricular obliteration, erythema, vocal fold/diffuse edema, posterior commissure hypertrophy, granuloma, thick mucus), 0-26; a score >7 suggests LPR.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9194976/)  {aka RFS}
Reflux Symptom Index (RSI) [concept · Laryngology · draft] — Nine-item patient questionnaire (each 0-5, max 45) scoring LPR symptoms; a score >13 is considered abnormal and suggestive of laryngopharyngeal reflux.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9702003/)  {aka RSI}
Zargar Endoscopic Classification [concept · Laryngology · draft] — Grades caustic mucosal injury: 0 normal; I edema/hyperemia; IIa superficial ulceration/exudate; IIb deep or circumferential ulcers; IIIa focal necrosis; IIIb extensive necrosis; IV perforation. Grade IIb and III predict stricture formation and higher morbidity/mortality.  (https://www.otoscape.com/eponyms/zargar-classification-of-caustic-esophageal-injury.html; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5421115/)  {aka Zargar grading}
Botulinum Toxin (OnabotulinumtoxinA) [drug · Laryngology · draft] — Cleaves SNAP-25 to block acetylcholine release at the neuromuscular junction. First-line for adductor spasmodic dysphonia via EMG-guided injection into the thyroarytenoid muscles (commonly ~1.25-2.5 units per side), with effects lasting ~3-4 months; also used for cricopharyngeal dysfunction and sialorrhea.  (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5712671/; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Botox, BoNT-A}
Proton Pump Inhibitor [drug · Laryngology · draft] — Irreversibly inhibits gastric H+/K+ ATPase; empiric once- to twice-daily PPI therapy for 2-3 months is first-line for LPR alongside behavioral/dietary reflux precautions. Benefit in isolated laryngeal symptoms is debated given a strong placebo effect.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka PPI, omeprazole}
External Branch of Superior Laryngeal Nerve [nerve · Laryngology · paired · draft] — Motor branch to the cricothyroid muscle, controlling vocal-fold lengthening and pitch. It runs alongside the superior thyroid pedicle and is at risk during ligation of the superior thyroid artery (Cernea classification); injury causes voice fatigue and loss of high-pitch (the 'voice of professional singers').  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Bailey's Head and Neck Surgery)  {aka external SLN, EBSLN}
Internal Branch of Superior Laryngeal Nerve [nerve · Laryngology · larynx · paired · draft] — Sensory branch piercing the thyrohyoid membrane to supply the supraglottic larynx; afferent limb of the laryngeal adductor (cough) reflex. Its injury causes aspiration from supraglottic anaesthesia. Landmark for a superior laryngeal nerve block.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka IBSLN}
Internal Branch of Superior Laryngeal Nerve [nerve · Laryngology · paired · draft] — Sensory branch that pierces the thyrohyoid membrane with the superior laryngeal vessels to supply mucosal sensation of the supraglottic larynx down to the vocal folds. Injury impairs the laryngeal adductor (cough) reflex and predisposes to aspiration.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Netter Atlas of Human Anatomy)  {aka internal SLN}
Superior Laryngeal Nerve [nerve · Laryngology · paired · draft] — Vagal branch dividing into an internal branch (supraglottic sensation, pierces the thyrohyoid membrane) and an external branch (motor to cricothyroid). External branch injury during thyroidectomy causes loss of high-pitch/vocal fatigue (the 'Amelita Galli-Curci' lesion).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SLN}
Caustic Ingestion [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Alkali causes liquefactive necrosis with deep penetration (worse esophageal injury/stricture), whereas acid causes coagulative necrosis. Do NOT induce emesis, give charcoal, or blindly pass a nasogastric tube; early flexible endoscopy grades injury. Airway assessment is paramount.  (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK557442/)  {aka caustic injury, corrosive ingestion}
Chronic (Neurogenic) Cough [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Cough >8 weeks; after excluding asthma, upper airway cough syndrome, and reflux, a refractory/neurogenic cough from laryngeal sensory neuropathy is treated with neuromodulators (gabapentin, amitriptyline) and behavioral cough-suppression therapy. Superior laryngeal nerve block is an emerging option.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka neurogenic cough, laryngeal sensory neuropathy}
Cricopharyngeal Dysfunction [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Impaired relaxation/opening of the upper esophageal sphincter producing a cricopharyngeal bar on esophagram and transfer dysphagia. Underlies Zenker formation and can be idiopathic, neurogenic, or reflux-related.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka cricopharyngeal achalasia, cricopharyngeal bar}
Esophageal Stricture [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Fibrotic luminal narrowing, classically a delayed sequela of caustic injury (weeks to months) or chronic reflux, presenting with progressive dysphagia to solids. Long-standing caustic strictures carry an increased lifetime risk of esophageal squamous cell carcinoma.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Essential Vocal Tremor [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Rhythmic 4-8 Hz oscillation of the voice present across tasks (including sustained vowels and at rest), often part of essential tremor. Distinguished from spasmodic dysphonia by its regular rhythm and lack of voice breaks.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka vocal tremor}
Laryngopharyngeal Reflux [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Retrograde flow of gastric contents reaching the larynx/pharynx causing hoarseness, globus, throat clearing, and cough; posterior laryngeal edema/erythema on exam. Unlike GERD, heartburn is often absent (upright/atypical reflux).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka LPR, reflux laryngitis}
Laryngotracheal / Subglottic Stenosis [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Narrowing at the cricoid/subglottis, most often acquired from prolonged intubation; other causes include GPA (granulomatosis with polyangiitis) and idiopathic subglottic stenosis in women. Graded by the Cotton-Myer system.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka subglottic stenosis, laryngotracheal stenosis, SGS}
Muscle Tension Dysphonia [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Functional hyperfunctional voice disorder with excessive laryngeal/supraglottic muscle recruitment and no primary neurologic lesion; unlike spasmodic dysphonia it is not task-specific and responds to voice therapy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka MTD}
Reinke's Edema (Polypoid Corditis) [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Diffuse gelatinous fluid accumulation in Reinke's space, typically bilateral and strongly associated with smoking; produces a low, gravelly voice. Management centers on smoking cessation with staged microflap reduction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka polypoid corditis, smoker's polyps}
Spasmodic Dysphonia [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Task-specific focal laryngeal dystonia. Adductor type (most common) gives a strained-strangled voice with glottal stops; abductor type gives breathy breaks. Symptoms lessen with singing/laughing, distinguishing it from muscle tension dysphonia.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka laryngeal dystonia, SD}
Vocal Fold Cyst [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Subepithelial mucus-retention or epidermoid cyst within the lamina propria that dampens the mucosal wave. Tends to resist voice therapy and needs microsurgical excision preserving the ligament.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vocal Fold Nodules [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Bilateral, symmetric fibrous thickenings at the midpoint of the membranous fold (junction of anterior and middle thirds) from phonotrauma. First-line treatment is voice therapy, not surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka singer's nodules, screamer's nodules}
Vocal Fold Paralysis [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Immobility from RLN/vagal injury; unilateral (breathy voice, aspiration) most often from thyroid/thoracic surgery or malignancy, bilateral (airway obstruction) after thyroidectomy. Must be distinguished from cricoarytenoid joint fixation by laryngeal EMG and palpation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka vocal cord paralysis, vocal fold immobility}
Vocal Fold Polyp [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — Usually unilateral exophytic lesion of the superficial lamina propria, often hemorrhagic from a single phonotraumatic event. Frequently requires phonomicrosurgery when it fails to resolve with voice rest and therapy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Zenker's Diverticulum [pathology · Laryngology · draft] — False (mucosal) pulsion diverticulum through Killian's dehiscence driven by cricopharyngeal dysfunction; presents with dysphagia, regurgitation of undigested food, halitosis, and aspiration in older adults. Diagnosed by barium esophagram.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka pharyngoesophageal diverticulum, Zenker}
Cricopharyngeal Myotomy [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Division of the cricopharyngeus (open or endoscopic) to relieve UES outflow obstruction; the key step in both open and endoscopic treatment of Zenker diverticulum and isolated cricopharyngeal dysfunction. Botulinum toxin injection is a less durable alternative/diagnostic test.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CP myotomy}
Cricotracheal Resection [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Open resection of the stenotic subglottic/cricoid segment with thyrotracheal anastomosis; definitive treatment for severe (Cotton-Myer III-IV) or refractory stenosis. Suprahyoid release and chin-to-chest positioning reduce anastomotic tension.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CTR, tracheal resection}
Endoscopic Airway Dilation [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Balloon or rigid dilation of a stenotic segment, often with radial CO2 laser incisions and adjunct topical/intralesional steroid or mitomycin-C. Best for thin, short (Cotton-Myer I-II) web-like stenoses; mature circumferential scar tends to recur and may need open repair.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka balloon dilation, endoscopic dilation}
Endoscopic Diverticulotomy (Stapler/Laser) [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Transoral division of the common wall between the diverticulum and esophagus (stapler or CO2 laser), simultaneously performing the cricopharyngeal myotomy. Faster recovery than open repair but requires adequate neck extension and a pouch large enough to expose.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Dohlman procedure, endoscopic stapling}
Esophageal Dilation [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Serial bougie or balloon dilation of a benign stricture, often after the caustic injury has matured (delayed several weeks to reduce perforation risk); refractory strictures may need steroid injection, stenting, or esophageal reconstruction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka bougie dilation, stricture dilation}
Esophagoscopy [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Flexible or rigid endoscopic evaluation of the esophagus to diagnose and grade injury, evaluate dysphagia/odynophagia, and remove foreign bodies. In caustic ingestion, early (within 12-24 h) endoscopy stages injury and guides management.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka esophagogastroduodenoscopy, EGD}
Injection Laryngoplasty [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Injection of a bulking agent (carboxymethylcellulose or hyaluronic acid temporarily, calcium hydroxylapatite longer-term) lateral to the vocalis to medialize the fold. Ideal early/temporary treatment when spontaneous recovery of the RLN is still possible.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka vocal fold injection, injection augmentation}
Laryngeal Electromyography [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Records intrinsic laryngeal muscle activity to distinguish neurogenic paralysis from cricoarytenoid joint fixation, prognosticate recovery of vocal fold paralysis, and guide targeted botulinum toxin injection in spasmodic dysphonia.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka LEMG}
Laryngeal Reinnervation [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Ansa cervicalis-to-RLN anastomosis restores muscle tone and bulk to a paralyzed fold without restoring purposeful movement; favored in younger patients with permanent unilateral paralysis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ansa-to-RLN reinnervation}
Laryngotracheal Reconstruction [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Expansion of the airway with autologous (costal) cartilage grafts placed via anterior and/or posterior cricoid split, done as single- or double-stage repair. Mainstay open technique for pediatric subglottic stenosis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka LTR, laryngotracheoplasty}
Medialization Laryngoplasty (Type I Thyroplasty) [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Isshiki type I thyroplasty places an implant (Silastic, Gore-Tex, or titanium) through a thyroid cartilage window to push a paralyzed fold medially. Permanent option for unilateral paralysis, often combined with arytenoid adduction for a large posterior glottic gap.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka type I thyroplasty, laryngeal framework surgery}
Phonomicrosurgery / Microflap Excision [procedure · Laryngology · draft] — Microlaryngoscopic elevation of a mucosal microflap to remove a benign lesion while preserving the vocal ligament and superficial lamina propria, protecting the mucosal wave. Cornerstone of surgical management for cysts and recalcitrant polyps.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka microflap, phonosurgery}

--- HEAD & NECK ONCOLOGY (101) ---
Accessory Parotid [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Detached islet of parotid tissue found in ~20% of people, lying on the masseter along Stensen's duct anterior to the main gland. Can be a site of tumor and must be addressed via a facelift/parotid approach, not a direct cheek incision.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Accessory parotid gland}
Base of Tongue [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · midline · draft] — Posterior third of the tongue (lingual tonsil), an oropharyngeal subsite. A frequent site of HPV-associated SCC and of occult primaries presenting as cystic level II nodes.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka BOT, Tongue base}
Buccinator Muscle [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Muscle of the cheek arising in part from the pterygomandibular raphe; compresses the cheek against the teeth during mastication.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Cervical Esophagus / Postcricoid Region [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · larynx · midline · draft] — Hypopharyngeal-esophageal junction; postcricoid carcinoma is associated with Plummer-Vinson syndrome. Circumferential defects here typically need jejunal or tubed free-flap reconstruction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Postcricoid region}
Cricopharyngeus Muscle [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · midline · draft] — Circular lower part of the inferior constrictor arising from the cricoid cartilage; functions as the upper esophageal sphincter (UES) and is the muscle divided in cricopharyngeal myotomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka upper esophageal sphincter}
Deep Lobe of Parotid [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Portion medial to the facial nerve plane, adjacent to the retromandibular vein and parapharyngeal space. Deep-lobe tumors can present as a dumbbell parapharyngeal mass and require total parotidectomy with facial nerve dissection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Medial lobe}
Floor of Mouth [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · oral-pharynx · midline · draft] — Oral cavity subsite bounded by the mandible and tongue; houses the submandibular ducts. FOM tumors can approach the mandible and periosteum, influencing marginal versus segmental mandibulectomy decisions.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka FOM}
Greater Palatine Foramen [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · foramen · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Opening at the posterolateral hard palate transmitting the greater palatine nerve and vessels that supply the mucosa of the hard palate.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Hard Palate [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · oral-pharynx · midline · draft] — Bony roof of the mouth formed by the palatine processes of the maxillae and horizontal plates of the palatine bones; separates the oral and nasal cavities.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)
Horizontal Plate of Palatine Bone [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Forms the posterior quarter of the hard palate; its posterior free border gives attachment to the palatine aponeurosis of the soft palate.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka horizontal plate of palatine}
Hyoid Bone [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · cervical-spine-hyoid · midline · draft] — U-shaped midline bone suspended by muscles and ligaments, anchoring the larynx and tongue base; the only bone with no direct articulation. Resected with the tract in Sistrunk procedure.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Incisive Foramen [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · foramen · oral-pharynx · midline · draft] — Midline opening behind the central incisors transmitting the nasopalatine nerve and sphenopalatine vessels to the anterior hard palate.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka incisive canal}
Inferior Pharyngeal Constrictor [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Lowest constrictor with thyropharyngeus (from the thyroid cartilage oblique line) and cricopharyngeus (from the cricoid cartilage) parts; the Killian dehiscence between them is the site of Zenker diverticulum.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)
Lateral Pterygoid Plate [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Broader plate of the sphenoid; its lateral and medial surfaces give origin to the lateral and medial pterygoid muscles respectively.  (Gray's Anatomy; Moore Clinically Oriented Anatomy)  {aka lateral pterygoid lamina}
Lesser Palatine Foramen [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · foramen · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Small opening posterior to the greater palatine foramen transmitting the lesser palatine nerves and vessels to the soft palate and tonsil.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Medial Pterygoid Plate [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Thin vertical plate of the sphenoid bone; its inferior tip curves laterally as the pterygoid hamulus, around which tensor veli palatini tendon hooks.  (Gray's Anatomy; Moore Clinically Oriented Anatomy)  {aka medial pterygoid lamina}
Middle Pharyngeal Constrictor [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Arises from the greater and lesser cornua of the hyoid bone and the stylohyoid ligament; overlaps the superior constrictor externally.  (Gray's Anatomy; Moore Clinically Oriented Anatomy)
Nasopharynx [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Space behind the nasal cavity above the soft palate; the fossa of Rosenmüller is the most common origin of NPC. Eustachian tube obstruction here causes unilateral serous otitis media in adults, a red flag.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Neck Level I (Submental/Submandibular) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · paired · draft] — Level Ia (submental) and Ib (submandibular); first-echelon drainage for the oral cavity, lip, and anterior tongue. Bounded by the mandible, digastric, and hyoid.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Level 1}
Neck Level II (Upper Jugular) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · paired · draft] — Upper jugular nodes from skull base to hyoid, split into IIa/IIb by the spinal accessory nerve. First-echelon drainage for oropharynx, larynx, and the most common site of cystic nodal metastasis in HPV+ disease.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Level 2}
Neck Level III (Middle Jugular) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · paired · draft] — Middle jugular nodes from hyoid to cricoid. Drains the oral cavity, oropharynx, hypopharynx, and larynx.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Level 3}
Neck Level IV (Lower Jugular) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · paired · draft] — Lower jugular nodes from cricoid to clavicle. Drains the hypopharynx, larynx, thyroid, and cervical esophagus; includes the Virchow node region.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Level 4}
Neck Level V (Posterior Triangle) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · paired · draft] — Posterior triangle nodes behind the sternocleidomastoid, split by the omohyoid. Drains the nasopharynx, posterior scalp, and neck skin; involved in nasopharyngeal and cutaneous malignancies.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Level 5}
Neck Level VI (Central Compartment) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · midline · draft] — Central compartment (pretracheal, paratracheal, prelaryngeal/Delphian) nodes between the carotid sheaths. Primary drainage for the thyroid, subglottis, and cervical esophagus.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Level 6, Central compartment}
Neck Level VII (Superior Mediastinal) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · midline · draft] — Superior mediastinal nodes below the suprasternal notch. Involved by subglottic, thyroid, cervical esophageal, and advanced hypopharyngeal cancers.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Level 7}
Oral Tongue (Anterior Two-Thirds) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · midline · draft] — Anterior two-thirds of the tongue, an oral cavity subsite; the lateral border is the most common site of oral SCC. Lymphatic drainage is primarily to levels I–III.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Anterior tongue}
Palatine Aponeurosis [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · membrane · oral-pharynx · midline · draft] — Fibrous sheet, the expanded tensor veli palatini tendon, attached to the posterior border of the hard palate; forms the fibrous skeleton of the soft palate into which the other palatal muscles insert.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka aponeurosis of soft palate}
Palatine Process of Maxilla [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Horizontal shelf of the maxilla forming the anterior three-quarters of the hard palate; meets its fellow at the median palatine suture.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Palatine Tonsil [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Lymphoid tissue in the tonsillar fossa between the palatoglossal and palatopharyngeal arches; the most common oropharyngeal subsite for HPV-associated SCC. Drains to levels II–III.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Tonsil}
Palatoglossal Arch (Anterior Tonsillar Pillar) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Anterior tonsillar pillar overlying the palatoglossus muscle; forms the anterior boundary of the tonsillar fossa and the oropharyngeal isthmus.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka anterior tonsillar pillar, palatoglossus}
Palatopharyngeal Arch (Posterior Tonsillar Pillar) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Posterior tonsillar pillar overlying the palatopharyngeus muscle; forms the posterior boundary of the tonsillar fossa.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka posterior tonsillar pillar, palatopharyngeus}
Parotid (Periparotid) Lymph Nodes [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · paired · draft] — Intra- and periparotid nodes that are the first-echelon basin for the anterior scalp, temple, and auricle. Frequently involved by cutaneous SCC and melanoma, often mandating parotidectomy plus neck dissection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Parotid Gland [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · neck · paired · draft] — Largest salivary gland, divided into superficial and deep lobes by the facial nerve. About 80% of salivary tumors arise here and most are benign; the facial nerve dictates surgical approach.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Pterygoid Hamulus [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Hook-shaped inferior projection of the medial pterygoid plate; the tensor veli palatini tendon turns around it, and the pterygomandibular raphe attaches superiorly here.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka hamulus}
Pterygomandibular Raphe [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · ligament · neck · paired · draft] — Tendinous band from the pterygoid hamulus to the posterior mylohyoid line where the buccinator meets the superior pharyngeal constrictor; landmark for inferior alveolar nerve block.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Pyriform Sinus [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · space · larynx · paired · draft] — Most common hypopharyngeal subsite, a recess lateral to the larynx. Rich lymphatics lead to early level II–IV nodal spread; medial-wall tumors can invade the larynx.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Piriform sinus, Pyriform fossa}
Stensen's Duct (Parotid Duct) [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Surface landmark: runs along a line from the mid-tragus to the mid-philtrum (middle third of that line). Pierces the buccinator to open opposite the maxillary second molar; buccal branch of VII runs parallel to it.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Parotid duct}
Stylohyoid Ligament [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · ligament · neck · paired · draft] — Fibrous band from the styloid tip to the lesser cornu of the hyoid (2nd branchial arch derivative); may ossify in Eagle syndrome.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Styloid Process [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Slender temporal bone projection anchoring the stylohyoid, stylomandibular and stylopharyngeus/styloglossus/stylohyoid muscles (Riolan's bouquet); elongation causes Eagle syndrome.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Superficial Lobe of Parotid [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — ~80% of parotid mass; lies lateral to the facial nerve plane. Superficial parotidectomy removes it while preserving the nerve — the standard operation for benign lateral-lobe tumors such as pleomorphic adenoma.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Lateral lobe}
Superior Pharyngeal Constrictor [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — Uppermost constrictor arising from the pterygoid hamulus, pterygomandibular raphe, and mandibular mylohyoid line; forms the lateral and posterior wall of the oropharynx.  (Gray's Anatomy; Cummings Otolaryngology)
True Vocal Fold [anatomy · Head & Neck Oncology · muscle · larynx · paired · draft] — Free edge of the glottis composed of the vocalis muscle, vocal ligament, and layered lamina propria. The paucity of lymphatics here underlies the favorable prognosis of early glottic cancer.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Vocal cord}
AJCC 8th Ed. HPV+ Oropharynx Staging [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — p16-positive OPSCC has its own staging: no Tis or T4b; clinical N by laterality/size (≤6 cm); pathologic N by node count (pN1 ≤4 nodes, pN2 >4). ENE is NOT part of HPV+ staging. Stage groupings shift so many node-positive patients are stage I–II, reflecting favorable survival.  (https://acsjournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.3322/caac.21389; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7410862/)
AJCC 8th Ed. Oral Cavity Staging [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — Incorporates depth of invasion into T and extranodal extension into N. Clinical/pathologic ENE upstages nodal disease (pN3b), reflecting its adverse prognostic weight in non-HPV mucosal SCC.  (AJCC Cancer Staging Manual 8th ed; https://acsjournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.3322/caac.21389)
Breslow Thickness [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · external-ear · draft] — Vertical tumor thickness in mm from the granular layer, the dominant T-category determinant for melanoma in AJCC 8th ed alongside ulceration. Guides excision margins and sentinel node decisions.  (AJCC Cancer Staging Manual 8th ed; https://iowaprotocols.medicine.uiowa.edu/protocols/melanoma-evaluation-and-management-8th-edition-ajcc)  {aka Breslow depth}
Concurrent Chemoradiation [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · draft] — Definitive or adjuvant platinum-based chemotherapy delivered concurrently with radiation to radiosensitize and treat micrometastases. Standard for advanced laryngeal (organ preservation), nasopharyngeal, and high-risk postoperative disease.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka CRT, Chemoradiotherapy}
Depth of Invasion (DOI) [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — AJCC 8th edition added DOI to oral cavity T-staging: DOI ≤5 mm, >5–10 mm, and >10 mm upstage T1→T2→T3 respectively. DOI (measured from the basement membrane) is distinct from surface tumor thickness.  (AJCC Cancer Staging Manual 8th ed; https://acsjournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.3322/caac.21389)  {aka DOI}
Epstein-Barr Virus (NPC) [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · nasal-sinus · draft] — EBV drives nonkeratinizing NPC; plasma EBV DNA serves as a biomarker for diagnosis, monitoring treatment response, and detecting recurrence in endemic populations.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka EBV}
Extranodal Extension (ENE) [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Tumor breach of the nodal capsule into perinodal tissue; a strong adverse prognosticator that upstages non-HPV nodal disease (pN3b) in AJCC 8th ed and, with positive margins, indicates adjuvant concurrent chemoradiation. Not incorporated into HPV+ oropharyngeal staging.  (https://acsjournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.3322/caac.21389; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36655591/)  {aka ENE, Extracapsular spread, ECS}
Head & Neck Radiation Toxicity [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · draft] — Acute mucositis and dermatitis and late xerostomia, dysphagia, trismus, hypothyroidism, and osteoradionecrosis of the mandible. IMRT and dental clearance before RT mitigate these; osteoradionecrosis risk drives pre-RT extraction planning.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Osteoradionecrosis, Xerostomia}
HPV / p16-Positive SCC [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — p16 immunohistochemistry is the surrogate marker for transcriptionally active HPV (usually type 16) in oropharyngeal SCC. p16-positive tumors have distinct biology, better radiosensitivity, and separate AJCC 8th staging.  (https://acsjournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.3322/caac.21389; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7410862/)  {aka p16-positive SCC, HPV-mediated SCC}
Laryngeal Organ Preservation [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · larynx · draft] — Concurrent cisplatin-based chemoradiation preserves the larynx in many advanced cases (RTOG 91-11), reserving total laryngectomy for salvage. Not appropriate for a nonfunctional larynx or extensive cartilage invasion (T4a), where upfront surgery gives better survival.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)
Nodal Sublevel IIb (Submuscular Recess) [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · paired · draft] — Upper jugular nodes superolateral (posterosuperior) to the spinal accessory nerve. Clearing IIb requires skeletonizing and retracting CN XI; dissect it only when indicated and avoid nerve traction to reduce shoulder morbidity.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Salivary FNA / Milan System [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Fine-needle aspiration is first-line for a salivary mass; the Milan System for Reporting Salivary Gland Cytopathology stratifies malignancy risk across six categories to guide surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Milan System, FNA}
SDHx Mutations [concept · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Germline succinate dehydrogenase subunit (SDHB/C/D) mutations underlie hereditary paraganglioma-pheochromocytoma syndromes; SDHB carries higher malignancy risk. Genetic testing is recommended for head/neck paraganglioma, especially multifocal or young patients.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka SDHB, SDHD}
Cetuximab [drug · Head & Neck Oncology · draft] — Anti-EGFR monoclonal antibody used as a radiosensitizer (Bonner trial) for patients unable to tolerate cisplatin. De-escalation trials (RTOG 1016/De-ESCALaTE) showed it is inferior to cisplatin in HPV+ oropharyngeal cancer, so it is not preferred there.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)
Cisplatin [drug · Head & Neck Oncology · draft] — Platinum radiosensitizer given concurrently with definitive/adjuvant RT (high-dose 100 mg/m² q3 weeks). Key toxicities are nephrotoxicity, ototoxicity, and neuropathy; indicated as adjuvant chemoradiation for ENE or positive margins.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)
Pembrolizumab [drug · Head & Neck Oncology · draft] — Anti-PD-1 checkpoint inhibitor. KEYNOTE-048 established first-line pembrolizumab (monotherapy if CPS ≥1, or with platinum/5-FU) for recurrent/metastatic HNSCC, improving overall survival versus the EXTREME regimen.  (https://ascopost.com/issues/june-25-2019/fda-approves-pembrolizumab-for-the-first-line-treatment-of-hnscc/; https://www.merck.com/news/keytruda-pembrolizumab-monotherapy-met-a-primary-endpoint-in-the-phase-3-keynote-048-trial-significantly-improving-os-as-first-line-therapy-in-head-and-neck-squamous-cell-carcinoma-patients-w/)  {aka Keytruda}
Ansa Cervicalis [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · paired · draft] — Motor loop of the cervical plexus (superior root C1 via hypoglossal, inferior root C2-C3) on the anterolateral carotid sheath; supplies the infrahyoid strap muscles. Its superior root can be traced to help identify the hypoglossal nerve and is a donor for laryngeal reinnervation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Auriculotemporal Nerve [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — V3 branch that encircles the middle meningeal artery, supplies the TMJ, external auditory canal and scalp, and conveys otic-ganglion parasympathetic secretomotor fibres to the parotid. Aberrant reinnervation after parotidectomy causes Frey syndrome (gustatory sweating).  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)  {aka ATN}
Brachial Plexus [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · paired · draft] — C5-T1 roots emerging between the anterior and middle scalenes and traversing the subclavian triangle deep to the prevertebral fascia. Encountered at the floor of a level V/Vb dissection — kept safe by not violating the prevertebral fascia.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cervical Plexus [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · paired · draft] — Sensory (C1-C4) plexus emerging at the posterior border of mid-SCM (Erb's point); gives great auricular, lesser occipital, transverse cervical and supraclavicular nerves plus the motor ansa cervicalis. Its cutaneous branches are commonly sacrificed in neck dissection, numbing the auricle/neck skin.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Cervical Sympathetic Chain [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · paired · draft] — Paravertebral chain of superior, middle and inferior (stellate) ganglia lying on longus colli, deep to the prevertebral/carotid sheath — medial and posterior to the carotid. Injury produces Horner syndrome (ptosis, miosis, anhidrosis); can be mistaken for the vagus in a parapharyngeal/retrostyloid dissection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka sympathetic trunk}
Great Auricular Nerve [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · paired · draft] — Largest ascending branch of the cervical plexus (C2-C3); curves around the posterior border of SCM to run superficial to it toward the parotid/auricle. A reliable landmark for the external jugular vein and for the nerve graft in facial reanimation; sacrifice numbs the lower ear lobule.  (Netter Atlas of Human Anatomy)
Lingual Nerve [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · paired · draft] — V3 branch carrying general sensation to the anterior two-thirds of tongue plus taste and parasympathetic fibres from chorda tympani. In the floor of mouth it loops under and crosses the submandibular duct (twice); protect it during submandibular gland excision and floor-of-mouth resection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)
Otic Ganglion [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · skull-base · paired · draft] — Parasympathetic ganglion below foramen ovale on the medial V3. Preganglionic input is the lesser petrosal nerve (from CN IX via the tympanic plexus); postganglionic secretomotor fibres hitchhike on the auriculotemporal nerve to the parotid - the basis of Frey syndrome after parotidectomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)
Phrenic Nerve [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · paired · draft] — C3-C4-C5 ('keeps the diaphragm alive'); descends vertically on the anterior surface of the anterior scalene, deep to the prevertebral fascia. Injury during posterior-triangle or supraclavicular dissection causes hemidiaphragm paralysis — keep dissection superficial to prevertebral fascia.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Submandibular Ganglion [nerve · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · paired · draft] — Parasympathetic ganglion suspended from the lingual nerve in the floor of mouth. Preganglionic fibres arrive via chorda tympani (CN VII); postganglionic fibres are secretomotor to the submandibular and sublingual glands. Encountered when the lingual nerve is dissected off the submandibular gland/duct.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Gray's Anatomy)
Adenoid Cystic Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Malignancy notorious for perineural invasion and late distant (lung) metastasis; cribriform, tubular, and solid patterns with the solid pattern most aggressive. Prone to skip lesions along nerves, complicating margin clearance.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ACC}
Carcinoma of Unknown Primary (Head & Neck) [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Metastatic cervical SCC with no identified mucosal source. Workup includes p16/EBV testing, PET-CT, exam under anesthesia, and TORS lingual/palatine tonsillectomy; p16+ level II nodes point to an occult oropharyngeal primary.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka CUP, Unknown primary}
Carotid Body Tumor [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Paraganglioma at the carotid bifurcation, splaying the internal and external carotid arteries (lyre sign). Shamblin classification grades encasement of the carotid and predicts surgical risk.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Chemodectoma}
Cutaneous Melanoma (Head & Neck) [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · external-ear · draft] — Head/neck (especially scalp) melanomas are thicker and more lethal than other sites. Wide local excision margins scale with Breslow thickness; sentinel node biopsy is considered for lesions ≥0.8 mm or with adverse features.  (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK572149/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10743441/)  {aka Melanoma}
Cutaneous Squamous Cell Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · external-ear · draft] — Common sun-exposed head/neck skin cancer. High-risk features include >2 mm thickness, perineural invasion, poor differentiation, ear/lip primary, and immunosuppression; these drive nodal metastasis to parotid and neck nodes.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11352224/)  {aka cSCC}
Frey Syndrome [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Gustatory sweating/flushing over the parotid skin after parotidectomy, from aberrant reinnervation of cutaneous sweat glands by auriculotemporal secretomotor fibers. Confirmed with the Minor starch-iodine test; prevented/treated with interposition grafts (SMAS, dermis) and botulinum toxin.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Gustatory sweating, Auriculotemporal syndrome}
Glomus Jugulare / Tympanicum [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · temporal-bone · draft] — Temporal bone paraganglioma of the jugular bulb (jugulare) or Jacobson nerve on the promontory (tympanicum). Presents with pulsatile tinnitus and a red retrotympanic mass; Fisch classification guides management.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Glomus tympanicum, Jugulotympanic paraganglioma}
Glottic Cancer [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · larynx · draft] — SCC of the true vocal folds; presents early with hoarseness and has sparse lymphatics, so early tumors (T1–T2) have excellent cure with single-modality radiation or transoral laser microsurgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Hypopharyngeal Squamous Cell Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · larynx · draft] — Aggressive SCC (pyriform sinus most common) with early submucosal spread and nodal metastasis, hence poor prognosis. Advanced disease often needs total laryngopharyngectomy with free-flap reconstruction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Laryngeal Squamous Cell Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · larynx · draft] — Strongly tobacco/alcohol related. Divided into glottic, supraglottic, and subglottic subsites with differing lymphatic drainage and prognosis. Organ-preservation chemoradiation competes with total laryngectomy for advanced disease.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Larynx cancer}
Mucoepidermoid Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Most common salivary malignancy (and most common pediatric salivary malignancy). Graded low to high; harbors MAML2 fusions. Low-grade tumors behave indolently while high-grade tumors metastasize.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka MEC}
Nasopharyngeal Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · nasal-sinus · draft] — EBV-associated (WHO type III nonkeratinizing) carcinoma endemic in Southern China. Presents with a neck mass, epistaxis, and serous otitis; radiosensitive, treated primarily with (chemo)radiation rather than surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka NPC}
Oral Cavity Squamous Cell Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — Most common oral cavity malignancy; risk factors tobacco/alcohol/betel nut. Oral tongue is the most common subsite. Elective neck dissection is favored when depth of invasion exceeds ~4 mm given occult nodal risk.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Oral SCC, OSCC}
Oropharyngeal Squamous Cell Carcinoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — Arises in tonsil, base of tongue, soft palate, posterior wall. HPV-positive disease now predominates and carries a markedly better prognosis than HPV-negative/tobacco-related tumors.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; https://acsjournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.3322/caac.21389)  {aka OPSCC}
Paraganglioma (Head & Neck) [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · skull-base · draft] — Neuroendocrine tumor of paraganglia (glomus tumors), usually benign but hypervascular. Classic salt-and-pepper appearance on MRI; a minority are catecholamine-secreting and require preoperative screening.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Glomus tumor}
Pleomorphic Adenoma [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Most common salivary neoplasm (benign mixed tumor) with epithelial and myxochondroid stroma. Requires complete excision with a cuff of normal tissue; enucleation risks recurrence, and long-standing lesions can undergo carcinoma ex pleomorphic adenoma transformation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Benign mixed tumor}
Supraglottic Cancer [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · larynx · draft] — SCC of epiglottis/false cords/aryepiglottic folds; rich bilateral lymphatics cause early, often bilateral, nodal metastasis, so elective treatment of both necks is standard.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Warthin Tumor [pathology · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Papillary cystadenoma lymphomatosum: benign, associated with smoking, can be bilateral/multifocal, and is essentially confined to the parotid. Shows avidity on technetium pertechnetate scans.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Papillary cystadenoma lymphomatosum}
Free Flap Reconstruction (Pharyngoesophageal) [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Microvascular reconstruction of pharyngoesophageal defects using radial forearm, anterolateral thigh (tubed), or jejunal free flaps to restore swallowing continuity after laryngopharyngectomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Free tissue transfer}
Glossectomy [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — Partial to total resection of the tongue for oral tongue SCC. Reconstruction ranges from primary closure to radial forearm or ALT free flaps depending on defect size; speech/swallow outcomes drive reconstructive choice.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Partial glossectomy}
Intensity-Modulated Radiation Therapy [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · draft] — Conformal RT that shapes dose to the target while sparing parotids and other organs at risk, reducing xerostomia. Standard technique for definitive and adjuvant head/neck radiation, typically ~70 Gy definitive.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka IMRT}
Mohs Micrographic Surgery [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · facial-skeleton · draft] — Staged excision with complete circumferential peripheral and deep margin assessment, maximizing cure and tissue conservation for high-risk facial cutaneous SCC and basal cell carcinoma.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Mohs}
Neck Dissection [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Systematic removal of cervical lymph node levels for staging and treatment. Classified as radical, modified radical, selective, and extended based on which levels and non-lymphatic structures (SCM, IJV, CN XI) are addressed.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)
Parotidectomy [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Superficial or total parotidectomy with facial nerve identification (via the tragal pointer, tympanomastoid suture, posterior belly of digastric). Frey syndrome and facial nerve weakness are key complications.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Preoperative Embolization [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Angiographic embolization of feeding vessels 24–48 h before resection of hypervascular tumors (paragangliomas, JNA) to reduce intraoperative blood loss.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Embolization}
Radical Neck Dissection [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Removal of levels I–V with sacrifice of the spinal accessory nerve, internal jugular vein, and sternocleidomastoid. Modified radical variants preserve one or more of these three structures to reduce shoulder morbidity.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka RND}
Selective Neck Dissection [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Preserves node levels not at risk while removing first-echelon basins (e.g., I–III for oral cavity, II–IV for larynx/pharynx). Standard for the N0 or low-burden neck; spares CN XI, IJV, and SCM.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SND}
Sentinel Lymph Node Biopsy [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · neck · draft] — Lymphatic mapping to identify the first-draining node, established for cutaneous melanoma and increasingly used in early oral cavity SCC to stage the clinically N0 neck while avoiding elective dissection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SLNB}
Total Laryngectomy [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · larynx · draft] — En bloc removal of the larynx with permanent tracheostoma, for advanced or radiation-failed disease. Voice rehabilitation via tracheoesophageal puncture and prosthesis is standard.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Laryngectomy}
Transoral Laser Microsurgery (TLM) [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · larynx · draft] — CO2-laser endoscopic resection of early glottic/supraglottic tumors, offering an organ-preserving alternative to radiation with the ability to re-treat and preserve future options.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka TLM}
Transoral Robotic Surgery (TORS) [procedure · Head & Neck Oncology · oral-pharynx · draft] — Robotic-assisted transoral resection of oropharyngeal tumors, allowing de-escalation of adjuvant therapy in select HPV+ cases. ORATOR/ORATOR2 compared primary TORS with primary radiotherapy; the surgical arm had more swallowing morbidity and, in ORATOR2, excess grade-5 toxicity, tempering enthusiasm.  (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC11121063/; https://www.ejcancer.com/article/S0959-8049(25)00124-8/abstract)  {aka TORS}
Retromandibular Vein [vessel · Head & Neck Oncology · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Formed by superficial temporal + maxillary veins within the gland. Lies just deep to the facial nerve — a reliable DEEP landmark for the nerve trunk and its divisions; the temporofacial/cervicofacial split lies lateral to it.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Posterior facial vein}
Thoracic Duct [vessel · Head & Neck Oncology · draft] — Terminates in the left venous angle (junction of internal jugular and subclavian veins) at the base of level IV; a smaller right lymphatic duct exists on the right. Injury during lower-neck dissection causes a chyle leak — inspect and ligate; manage with low-fat/MCT diet or re-exploration.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)

--- FACIAL PLASTICS (114) ---
Alveolar Process of Maxilla [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Tooth-bearing inferior ridge of the maxilla; its dental sockets anchor the vertical midface buttresses and are disrupted in Le Fort I fractures.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Coronoid Process [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Anterosuperior ramus process giving insertion to the temporalis muscle; separated from the condyle by the mandibular (sigmoid) notch.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Depressor Anguli Oris [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — From the mandible to the modiolus; depresses the oral commissure (frown). Innervated by the marginal mandibular branch of VII — its unopposed contralateral pull causes the classic asymmetry when the marginal branch is injured.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka DAO}
Facial Buttresses [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Thickened bony struts that transmit masticatory forces and dictate rigid fixation in midface trauma. Vertical: nasomaxillary (medial), zygomaticomaxillary (lateral), pterygomaxillary, and vertical mandible. Horizontal: frontal bar, infraorbital rim, maxillary alveolus/palate. Restoring buttresses restores facial height, width, and projection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka midface buttresses}
Frontal Bone [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Forms the forehead, supraorbital rims and orbital roofs and houses the frontal sinus; its inferior articulations define the frontal-bar horizontal buttress.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Frontal Process of Maxilla [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Superomedial maxillary projection meeting the frontal and nasal bones; forms the medial orbital rim and part of the nasomaxillary buttress and lacrimal fossa.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Frontalis [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Frontal belly of occipitofrontalis; elevates brow and wrinkles forehead. Sole innervation from the temporal (frontal) branch of VII, which has minimal cross-innervation — injury causes permanent brow ptosis.  (Gray's Anatomy)  {aka Frontal belly}
Frontomaxillary Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Suture between the frontal bone and the frontal process of the maxilla, superior to the medial orbital rim.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Frontonasal Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Midline articulation of the nasal bones with the frontal bone; its midpoint (nasion) is a standard cephalometric landmark.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Frontozygomatic Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Articulation of the zygomatic frontal process with the frontal bone; a key reduction/fixation point and vertical buttress landmark in ZMC fracture repair.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Glabella [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Smooth midline prominence of the frontal bone between the supraorbital ridges and above the frontonasal suture; a cephalometric soft-tissue landmark.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Glenoid (Mandibular) Fossa [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Concavity on the temporal bone squama that receives the mandibular condyle and articular disc to form the TMJ; bounded anteriorly by the articular eminence.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Horizontal Plate of Palatine Bone [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Forms the posterior one-quarter of the hard palate, meeting the palatine process of the maxilla anteriorly and its fellow at the midline.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Intermaxillary Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Midline suture uniting the two maxillae along the palatine processes and anterior nasal spine; site of the median palatal raphe.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Internasal Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Midline suture joining the paired nasal bones forming the bony dorsum ridge.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Lacrimal Bone [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Small medial orbital wall bone forming, with the maxillary frontal process, the lacrimal fossa lodging the lacrimal sac and nasolacrimal duct.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Lateral Pterygoid [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Two heads from the greater wing/lateral pterygoid plate to the condylar neck and TMJ disc; protrudes the mandible and opens/deviates the jaw. Innervated by V3 — its pull displaces high condylar fractures anteromedially.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Lower Lateral (Alar) Cartilage [anatomy · Facial Plastics · cartilage · nasal-sinus · paired · draft] — Paired U-shaped tip cartilages with medial, middle (domal), and lateral crura defining tip shape, projection, and rotation. Their intrinsic strength and attachments are major tip-support mechanisms; cephalic trim and suture techniques refine the tip.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka alar cartilage, LLC}
Mandible [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — U-shaped lower facial bone; fracture subsites are condyle/subcondyle, ramus, angle, body, parasymphysis, and symphysis. Muscle vectors (masseter, pterygoids, mylohyoid, digastric) determine whether a fracture is favorable or unfavorable to displacement. Bears the lower dental arch.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka lower jaw}
Mandibular Alveolar Ridge [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Tooth-bearing crest of the mandibular body; resorbs after tooth loss and is relevant to dental rehabilitation and osteotomy planning.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Mandibular Angle [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Junction of ramus and body; a frequent fracture site, weakened by the third molar and the masseter-medial pterygoid muscular sling.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Mandibular Body [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Horizontal tooth-bearing segment transmitting the inferior alveolar nerve through its canal to the mental foramen.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Mandibular Condyle [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Rounded superior process of the ramus articulating with the temporal glenoid fossa at the TMJ; common site of subcondylar fracture.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Mandibular Ramus [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Vertical posterior segment bearing the condyle and coronoid; lateral surface receives masseter, medial surface receives medial pterygoid.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Mandibular Symphysis [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Midline anterior mandible (fused embryologic symphysis); parasymphyseal fractures often accompany contralateral condylar fractures.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Masseter [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Powerful elevator of the mandible (jaw closure) running from the zygomatic arch to the lateral ramus/angle. Innervated by the masseteric nerve from V3 — a common donor for smile reanimation nerve transfers.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Maxilla [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Paired central midface bone forming the upper jaw, anterior maxillary sinus wall, inferior orbital rim/floor, piriform aperture and hard palate; key to Le Fort fracture patterns.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Medial Pterygoid [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — From the pterygoid plate/fossa to the medial angle of the mandible; elevates the jaw and mirrors the masseter internally (pterygomasseteric sling). Innervated by V3. Trismus after infiltration reflects its involvement.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Nasal Aesthetic Subunits [anatomy · Facial Plastics · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Nine topographic subunits (dorsum, paired sidewalls, tip, paired alae, columella, paired soft-triangles). Burget-Menick subunit principle: when a defect involves >50% of a convex subunit, resurface the entire subunit so scars fall in the shadowed borders.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka subunit principle}
Nasal Bone [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Paired bones forming the bony nasal dorsum; articulate superiorly with frontal bone, medially with each other, laterally with the maxillary frontal process.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Nasal Septum [anatomy · Facial Plastics · cartilage · nasal-sinus · midline · draft] — Midline partition of quadrangular (septal) cartilage plus perpendicular plate of ethmoid and vomer. Caudal septum is a major tip-support structure and the source of septal cartilage grafts (spreader, columellar strut). L-strut of ≥10-15 mm dorsal and caudal must be preserved to avoid saddle deformity.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka septal cartilage}
Nasomaxillary Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Suture between the lateral edge of the nasal bone and the frontal process of the maxilla, along the nasomaxillary buttress.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Orbicularis Oculi [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Sphincter of the eyelids; closes the eye and drives the blink/lacrimal pump. Innervated by temporal and zygomatic branches of VII — paralysis produces lagophthalmos and exposure keratopathy.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Orbicularis Oris [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Sphincter of the mouth; closes and purses the lips for oral competence and speech. Innervated by buccal and marginal mandibular branches of VII.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Orbital Floor [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Thin maxillary/zygomatic plate over the maxillary sinus, weakest posteromedially. Herniation or entrapment here produces enophthalmos, hypoglobus, and restricted upgaze; the infraorbital nerve runs in its canal, causing cheek numbness when injured.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka orbital rim floor}
Palatine Bone [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — L-shaped bone with horizontal and perpendicular plates; forms the posterior hard palate, lateral nasal wall and part of the pterygopalatine fossa.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Palatine Process of Maxilla [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Horizontal shelf forming the anterior three-quarters of the hard palate, meeting its fellow at the intermaxillary suture and the palatine bone posteriorly.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Perpendicular Plate of Palatine Bone [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Vertical plate forming the posterior lateral nasal wall; its sphenopalatine notch, closed by the sphenoid, becomes the sphenopalatine foramen.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Pes Anserinus [anatomy · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — The "goose's foot" — the branching point where the extratemporal facial nerve trunk splits into the temporofacial (upper) and cervicofacial (lower) divisions within the parotid, typically ~1.3 cm from the stylomastoid foramen.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Pes anserinus of facial nerve}
Platysma [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · neck · paired · draft] — Broad subcutaneous sheet of the neck/lower face, part of the SMAS. Depresses the mandible/lower lip and tenses neck skin. Innervated by the cervical branch of VII — its flap is elevated first in parotid and neck surgery.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Pterygomaxillary Fissure [anatomy · Facial Plastics · foramen · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Cleft between the maxilla and the pterygoid process leading into the pterygopalatine fossa; transmits the terminal maxillary artery and posterior superior alveolar branches; landmark in maxillary (Le Fort) osteotomy and JNA resection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Sphenomandibular Ligament [anatomy · Facial Plastics · ligament · neck · paired · draft] — Accessory TMJ ligament from the sphenoid spine to the mandibular lingula (Meckel's cartilage remnant); primary passive support of the mandible and a landmark shielding the inferior alveolar nerve at the mandibular foramen.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Stylomandibular Ligament [anatomy · Facial Plastics · ligament · neck · paired · draft] — Thickened deep cervical fascia from the styloid process to the mandibular angle; accessory TMJ ligament and the boundary separating the parotid from the submandibular space.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Superficial Musculoaponeurotic System (SMAS) [anatomy · Facial Plastics · fascia · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Continuous fibromuscular fascial layer investing the mimetic muscles, continuous with the platysma inferiorly and the temporoparietal fascia superiorly. It is the key structural plane manipulated (plication, imbrication, deep-plane dissection) in rhytidectomy; the facial nerve branches run deep to it.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka SMAS}
Supraorbital Rim [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Superior orbital margin of the frontal bone bearing the supraorbital notch/foramen; part of the upper transverse (frontal-bar) midface buttress.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Temporalis [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Fan-shaped muscle from the temporal fossa inserting on the coronoid process; elevates and retracts the mandible. Innervated by deep temporal nerves of V3; its tendon/flap is used in temporalis transfer for dynamic reanimation.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Temporomandibular Joint (TMJ) [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Bilateral synovial ginglymoarthrodial joint between the mandibular condyle and the glenoid fossa of the temporal bone; the only joint with a fibrocartilaginous articular disc dividing it into upper (translation) and lower (rotation) compartments.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
TMJ Articular Disc (Meniscus) [anatomy · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Biconcave fibrocartilage separating the TMJ into superior and inferior compartments; anterior displacement produces internal derangement and clicking.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Tragal Pointer [anatomy · Facial Plastics · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — Surgical landmark for the facial nerve trunk in parotidectomy: the pointed medial end of tragal cartilage. The nerve lies roughly 1 cm deep and inferior/anteromedial to the pointer - a soft landmark, so use it with the tympanomastoid suture and posterior digastric belly.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka tragal cartilage pointer}
Tympanomastoid Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — The most reliable landmark for the extratemporal facial nerve trunk: the nerve emerges from the stylomastoid foramen roughly 6-8 mm deep to the inferior end of this suture line between the tympanic and mastoid bone.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka tympanomastoid fissure}
Zygoma [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Malar bone forming cheek prominence and lateral orbital rim/floor; articulates with frontal, temporal, maxillary and sphenoid bones as the ZMC quadripod.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Zygomatic Process of Maxilla [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Lateral maxillary projection articulating with the zygoma at the zygomaticomaxillary suture; component of the lateral (zygomaticomaxillary) vertical buttress.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Zygomaticomaxillary Complex (ZMC) [anatomy · Facial Plastics · bone · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — The malar 'tetrapod' articulating at zygomaticofrontal, zygomaticomaxillary (infraorbital rim), zygomaticotemporal (arch), and zygomaticosphenoid sutures. Determines malar projection and facial width; the zygomaticosphenoid suture at the lateral orbital wall is the key intraoperative gauge of reduction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka malar complex, tripod}
Zygomaticomaxillary Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Junction of the zygoma and maxilla along the inferior orbital rim/zygomaticomaxillary buttress; step-off here signals ZMC fracture.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Zygomaticotemporal Suture [anatomy · Facial Plastics · joint · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Articulation of the zygomatic process of the temporal bone with the temporal process of the zygoma forming the zygomatic arch.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Zygomaticus Major [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Runs from zygomatic bone to the modiolus; principal smile elevator drawing the oral commissure up and back. Key target for smile reanimation (e.g., masseteric-to-buccal transfer). Innervated by zygomatic/buccal branches of VII.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Zygomaticus Minor [anatomy · Facial Plastics · muscle · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — From zygomatic bone to the upper lip; elevates the lip and deepens the nasolabial fold. Innervated by zygomatic/buccal branches of VII.  (Gray's Anatomy)
Facial Danger Zones [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Regions of superficial nerve vulnerability: temporal (frontal) branch of CN VII along Pitanguy's line, marginal mandibular branch near the mandibular border, the great auricular nerve over the sternocleidomastoid (most commonly injured in facelift), and the spinal accessory nerve in the posterior triangle. Knowledge prevents iatrogenic palsy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka danger zones of the face}
Facial Nerve Electrodiagnostics (ENoG/EMG) [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Electroneuronography (ENoG) compares evoked CMAP amplitude to the normal side; >90% degeneration within 14 days of complete palsy is a surgical decompression threshold. EMG detects voluntary motor units and reinnervation (polyphasic potentials), guiding prognosis and reanimation candidacy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ENoG, electromyography}
Frontal Bar (Upper Transverse) Buttress [concept · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Thick horizontal bar of the frontal bone across the supraorbital rims and glabella; the superior transverse strut resisting midface disruption.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
House-Brackmann Grading Scale [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Six-grade clinician scale for facial nerve function: I normal, II mild dysfunction, III moderate (complete eye closure with effort, obvious asymmetry), IV moderate-severe (incomplete eye closure), V severe (barely perceptible motion), VI total paralysis. Grade III/IV is the common threshold for intervention decisions.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka HB scale}
Infraorbital Rim (Transverse) Buttress [concept · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Horizontal buttress along the inferior orbital rim linking the medial and lateral vertical struts; commonly plated in ZMC and orbital fractures.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Le Fort Fracture Classification [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Midface fracture patterns all crossing the pterygoid plates: Le Fort I (transverse, palate-separating 'floating palate'), Le Fort II (pyramidal, through infraorbital rims and nasofrontal region), Le Fort III (craniofacial disjunction through zygomaticofrontal sutures and orbits). Produce malocclusion and midface mobility.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Le Fort I II III}
Microvascular Anastomosis & Free Flap Monitoring [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Free tissue transfer requires microsurgical arterial and venous anastomosis to neck recipient vessels (commonly facial artery, external jugular/IJ branches). Postoperative monitoring (clinical color/turgor/capillary refill, implantable Doppler) detects venous congestion or arterial thrombosis early; salvage depends on prompt re-exploration.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka flap monitoring, microsurgery}
Nasal Analysis & Aesthetics [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Systematic assessment of nasofrontal angle (115-130°), nasolabial angle (90-100° men, 95-115° women), tip projection (Goode ratio ~0.55-0.60), rotation, and dorsal aesthetic lines. Guides operative planning and preserves airway function.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka facial analysis nose}
Nasomaxillary (Medial) Buttress [concept · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Medial vertical midface buttress running along the piriform aperture from the alveolus through the frontal process of the maxilla to the frontal bone.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Palatoalveolar (Lower Transverse) Buttress [concept · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · midline · draft] — Horizontal palatal-alveolar strut across the hard palate tying the paired vertical buttresses together at the occlusal level.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Pitanguy Line [concept · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Surface guide to the temporal (frontal) branch of the facial nerve: a line from 0.5 cm below the tragus to 1.5 cm above the lateral eyebrow. The branch crosses the zygomatic arch deep to SMAS/temporoparietal fascia along this line and must be protected in facelift and browlift.  (Facial Plastic and Reconstructive Surgery (Papel); AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Pitanguy's line}
Pterygomaxillary (Posterior) Buttress [concept · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Posterior vertical buttress connecting the maxilla to the sphenoid pterygoid plates; disrupted in Le Fort fractures via the pterygomaxillary junction.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Relaxed Skin Tension Lines (RSTL) [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Lines of minimal resting skin tension, generally perpendicular to underlying muscle vectors. Orienting incisions and scar revisions (Z-plasty, W-plasty) parallel to RSTL and along aesthetic subunit borders minimizes scar visibility and wound tension.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka RSTL, Langer's lines}
Sunnybrook Facial Grading System [concept · Facial Plastics · draft] — Regional, weighted composite score (0-100) assessing resting symmetry, voluntary movement across five expressions, and synkinesis. More sensitive than House-Brackmann for tracking longitudinal change and detecting synkinesis after recovery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Sunnybrook score}
Zygomaticomaxillary (Lateral) Buttress [concept · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Lateral vertical buttress from the maxillary alveolus through the zygomaticomaxillary junction and zygoma to the frontal bone; a key ORIF fixation strut.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Buccal Branch of Facial Nerve [nerve · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Terminal facial branch to buccinator, orbicularis oris and midface elevators; runs near the parotid duct (parallel/just above it) - the duct is a surgical landmark to find it during parotidectomy. Extensive cross-innervation with zygomatic branch.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Facial Plastic and Reconstructive Surgery (Papel))
Cervical Branch of Facial Nerve [nerve · Facial Plastics · neck · paired · draft] — Terminal facial branch descending into the neck to innervate platysma; can be mistaken for the marginal mandibular branch but weakening it only affects the platysma/lower-lip depressor synergy. Exits the parotid tail near the angle of the mandible.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Facial Plastic and Reconstructive Surgery (Papel))
Cervicofacial Division (VII) [nerve · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Lower division from the pes anserinus giving (buccal), marginal mandibular and cervical branches. Supplies the perioral and platysmal musculature; the marginal mandibular branch it carries is the classic danger nerve of the lower face.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Lower division of facial nerve}
Facial Nerve Segments [nerve · Facial Plastics · temporal-bone · paired · draft] — CN VII courses through six segments: intracranial, meatal, labyrinthine (narrowest, gives GSPN at geniculate ganglion), tympanic, mastoid (gives nerve to stapedius and chorda tympani), and extratemporal (branches within parotid into temporal, zygomatic, buccal, marginal mandibular, cervical). Pes anserinus at stylomastoid foramen.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka CN VII anatomy, facial nerve course}
Inferior Alveolar Nerve [nerve · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — V3 branch entering the mandibular foramen to supply lower teeth, exiting the mental foramen as the mental nerve. At risk in mandibular fractures, third-molar surgery, sagittal split osteotomy and marginal mandibulectomy; injury causes lower-lip and chin numbness.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka IAN}
Infraorbital Nerve [nerve · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Terminal V2 branch running in the orbital floor and exiting the infraorbital foramen to supply the cheek, lower eyelid, lateral nose and upper lip. Commonly injured/entrapped in orbital-floor (blowout) and zygomaticomaxillary-complex fractures.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka ION}
Marginal Mandibular Branch (VII) [nerve · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Danger nerve of the lower face. Runs deep to platysma; may dip below the mandibular border (posteriorly it can lie ~1–2 cm below) before crossing the facial vessels. Protected by ligating the facial vein and raising the flap deep to it (Hayes-Martin maneuver). Injury causes lower-lip weakness.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Marginal mandibular nerve, MMN}
Marginal Mandibular Branch of Facial Nerve [nerve · Facial Plastics · neck · paired · draft] — Terminal facial branch to the depressors of the lower lip. In up to 20% it dips below the mandibular border; it runs deep to platysma and superficial to the facial vessels. Protect it during submandibular gland excision by ligating the facial vein low and elevating the flap above the gland (Hayes-Martin manoeuvre).  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Facial Plastic and Reconstructive Surgery (Papel))  {aka marginal mandibular nerve, MMN}
Temporal (Frontal) Branch of Facial Nerve [nerve · Facial Plastics · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Terminal facial branch to frontalis, corrugator and orbicularis oculi (upper). Runs within the temporoparietal fascia along the Pitanguy line; considered a 'terminal' branch with little cross-innervation, so injury causes permanent brow ptosis. Deep to SMAS over the zygomatic arch.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; Facial Plastic and Reconstructive Surgery (Papel))  {aka frontal branch}
