Superior Orbital Fissure [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · foramen · skull-base · paired · draft] — Cleft between sphenoid wings transmitting CN III, IV, VI, V1 (ophthalmic) and the superior ophthalmic vein; involved in superior orbital fissure syndrome.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Thyrohyoid Muscle [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · muscle · neck · paired · draft] — Infrahyoid strap from the thyroid cartilage to the hyoid; depresses the hyoid or elevates the larynx when the hyoid is fixed. Innervated by C1 fibers travelling with the hypoglossal nerve.  (Moore Clinically Oriented Anatomy)  {aka strap muscle}
Trachea [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · midline · draft] — Fibrocartilaginous airway of ~18-22 C-shaped rings beginning at the cricoid. The cricothyroid membrane (below the thyroid, above the cricoid) is the surface landmark for emergency cricothyrotomy; tracheostomy is placed through the 2nd-3rd rings, below the isthmus.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Transverse Foramen (Foramen Transversarium) [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · foramen · cervical-spine-hyoid · paired · draft] — Foramen in the cervical transverse process transmitting the vertebral artery and vein (artery typically enters at C6 and ascends to C1). A defining feature of cervical vertebrae.  (Moore Clinically Oriented Anatomy)  {aka foramen transversarium}
Transverse Process (Cervical) [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · bone · cervical-spine-hyoid · paired · draft] — Lateral bony process of a cervical vertebra containing the transverse foramen; anterior tubercle of C6 (carotid tubercle of Chassaignac) is a compression landmark for the common carotid artery.  (Moore Clinically Oriented Anatomy)
Trapezius Muscle [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · muscle · neck · paired · draft] — Forms the posterior border of the posterior triangle; innervated by the spinal accessory nerve (CN XI) with C3-C4 proprioceptive contribution. Denervation causes shoulder syndrome — lateral scapular winging, drooped shoulder and painful abduction.  (Netter Atlas of Human Anatomy)
Trigeminal Nerve (CN V) [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · skull-base · paired · draft] — Largest cranial nerve; divides into V1 (ophthalmic), V2 (maxillary) and V3 (mandibular), each exiting the skull base through a separate foramen.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vagus Nerve (CN X) [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · skull-base · paired · draft] — CN X leaves the skull through the jugular foramen and descends in the carotid sheath; supplies laryngeal and pharyngeal function.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Visceral (Pretracheal) Space [anatomy · Fundamentals/Anatomy · space · neck · midline · draft] — Midline compartment enclosed by the pretracheal/visceral fascia containing the thyroid gland, trachea, and esophagus; infection can descend to the anterior superior mediastinum.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka pretracheal space, anterior visceral space}
Anterior Cervical Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · paired · draft] — Bounded by inferior border of mandible (superior), anterior border of sternocleidomastoid (posterior) and the anterior midline of the neck. Subdivided by the digastric and omohyoid into submental, submandibular, carotid and muscular triangles.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka anterior triangle}
Carotid Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · paired · draft] — Bounded by superior belly of omohyoid (anteroinferior), posterior belly of digastric (superior) and anterior border of SCM (posterior). Contains the carotid bifurcation, internal jugular vein, vagus and hypoglossal nerves and the ansa cervicalis — the classic surgical exposure of the great vessels.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Coagulation Cascade [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Intrinsic (PTT-monitored), extrinsic (PT/INR-monitored) and common pathways converging on thrombin and fibrin. Platelet plug formation (primary hemostasis) precedes the fibrin clot (secondary hemostasis); vitamin K-dependent factors are II, VII, IX, X.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka clotting cascade, hemostasis pathway}
Computed Tomography (CT) [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — X-ray cross-sectional imaging that is the workhorse for bony detail, sinonasal/temporal bone anatomy and deep neck infection. Contrast enhancement (iodinated) highlights vessels and ring-enhancing abscesses; Hounsfield units quantify tissue density.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka CT scan, CAT scan}
Difficult Airway Algorithm [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — ASA framework for anticipated/unanticipated difficult airways emphasizing awake intubation when difficulty is predicted, maintaining oxygenation, limiting attempts, calling for help, and progressing to supraglottic rescue then surgical (front-of-neck) access. 'Cannot intubate, cannot oxygenate' triggers cricothyrotomy.  (ASA Difficult Airway Guidelines; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka ASA difficult airway, CICO}
Likelihood Ratios [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — LR+ = sensitivity/(1−specificity); LR− = (1−sensitivity)/specificity. Prevalence-independent; combined with pretest odds they yield post-test probability. LR+ >10 or LR− <0.1 substantially change management.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka LR+, LR-}
Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Superior soft-tissue and neural imaging using T1/T2 sequences; preferred for skull base, perineural spread, parotid/parapharyngeal tumors and vestibular schwannoma. Gadolinium enhances vascular/inflammatory tissue; DWI aids cholesteatoma and abscess detection.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka MR imaging}
Muscular (Omotracheal) Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · paired · draft] — Bounded by superior belly of omohyoid (superolateral), anterior border of SCM (inferolateral) and the midline. Contains the infrahyoid strap muscles overlying the thyroid gland, larynx and trachea — the access corridor for thyroidectomy and central-compartment (level VI) dissection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Occipital Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · paired · draft] — Superior subdivision of the posterior triangle, above the inferior belly of omohyoid. The spinal accessory nerve crosses its roof, exiting the posterior border of SCM near Erb's point — the principal danger point of level V dissection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
P-Value & Confidence Interval [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — The p-value is the probability of observing data as/more extreme under the null hypothesis (conventionally significant at <0.05); a 95% confidence interval conveys precision and, if it excludes the null (1 for ratios, 0 for differences), indicates significance. Type I error = false positive, Type II = false negative (1−power).  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka p value, confidence interval, CI, type I error, type II error}
Perioperative Anticoagulation Management [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Balancing thromboembolic vs bleeding risk when holding/bridging warfarin, DOACs and antiplatelets around surgery. Warfarin held ~5 days (INR normalization), DOACs 1–3 days by renal function; bridging with heparin reserved for high-risk indications.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka anticoagulation bridging, warfarin, DOAC}
PET/CT (FDG) [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Fluorodeoxyglucose PET fused with CT quantifies metabolic activity (SUV) for cancer staging, unknown primary workup and post-treatment surveillance. Best performed ≥12 weeks after chemoradiation to reduce false positives from inflammation.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka FDG-PET, PET scan}
Positive & Negative Predictive Value [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — PPV = true positives / all test-positives; NPV = true negatives / all test-negatives. Unlike sensitivity/specificity, predictive values depend on disease prevalence: PPV rises and NPV falls as prevalence increases.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka PPV, NPV}
Posterior Cervical Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · paired · draft] — Bounded by posterior border of SCM (anterior), anterior border of trapezius (posterior) and the middle third of clavicle (inferior). The inferior omohyoid belly splits it into occipital and subclavian (supraclavicular) triangles; corresponds to nodal level V.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka posterior triangle}
Rapid Sequence Induction [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Induction technique for aspiration-risk patients using preoxygenation, a rapid-onset induction agent and a fast-acting paralytic (succinylcholine or rocuronium) with minimal bag-mask ventilation to secure the airway quickly.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka RSI}
Sensitivity & Specificity [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Sensitivity = true positives / all diseased (SnNout: a sensitive test that is negative rules out disease). Specificity = true negatives / all non-diseased (SpPin: a specific test that is positive rules in). Both are intrinsic to the test and independent of prevalence.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Sn, Sp, SnNout, SpPin}
Study Designs & Levels of Evidence [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Hierarchy from case reports/series up through case-control (odds ratio), cohort (relative risk), randomized controlled trials, and systematic reviews/meta-analyses at the apex. RCTs minimize bias via randomization and blinding; observational designs suit rare/prognostic questions.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka levels of evidence, RCT, cohort, case-control}
Subclavian (Supraclavicular/Omoclavicular) Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · paired · draft] — Inferior subdivision of the posterior triangle, below the omohyoid inferior belly, above the clavicle. Contains the third part of the subclavian artery, brachial plexus trunks, external jugular vein and (on the left) the thoracic duct terminus.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka supraclavicular triangle, omoclavicular triangle}
Submandibular (Digastric) Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · paired · draft] — Bounded by anterior and posterior bellies of digastric and the inferior border of the mandible. Contains the submandibular gland, facial vessels, and the lingual, hypoglossal and marginal mandibular nerves — the key at-risk structures during gland excision (level IB).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka digastric triangle}
Submental Triangle [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · midline · draft] — Suprahyoid midline triangle bounded by the two anterior bellies of digastric laterally and the hyoid inferiorly; floor is mylohyoid. Contains submental (level IA) nodes; drains anterior floor of mouth, lower lip and tongue tip.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Surgical Antibiotic Prophylaxis [concept · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Timing (within 60 min of incision), agent (cefazolin ± metronidazole; add anaerobic/gram-negative coverage for clean-contaminated mucosal head and neck cases) and redosing based on surgical wound classification. Prophylaxis not routinely indicated for clean cases like thyroidectomy.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka perioperative antibiotics, SCIP prophylaxis}
Amoxicillin-Clavulanate [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Aminopenicillin plus beta-lactamase inhibitor; first-line for acute bacterial rhinosinusitis, acute otitis media (high-dose) and odontogenic infections, covering S. pneumoniae, H. influenzae, M. catarrhalis and oral anaerobes.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Augmentin, co-amoxiclav}
Ampicillin-Sulbactam [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — IV beta-lactam/beta-lactamase inhibitor combination that is a mainstay empiric therapy for deep neck space infections, covering streptococci, oral anaerobes and many gram-negatives.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka Unasyn}
Clindamycin [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Lincosamide with strong anaerobic and gram-positive coverage; used for penicillin-allergic patients with odontogenic/deep neck infections and as MRSA option. Risk of Clostridioides difficile colitis; rising anaerobic resistance noted.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)
Gadolinium Contrast [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Paramagnetic MRI contrast agent that shortens T1, enhancing vascular and inflammatory tissue. Risk of nephrogenic systemic fibrosis in severe renal failure limits use; newer macrocyclic agents are more stable.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka gadolinium, MRI contrast}
Iodinated Contrast [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Intravenous CT contrast that enhances vessels and inflammatory rims. Cautions include contrast-induced nephropathy in renal impairment, allergic-type reactions, and interaction with metformin; thyroid iodine load can affect subsequent RAI therapy.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka CT contrast}
Local Anesthetics [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Amides (lidocaine, bupivacaine) and esters that block voltage-gated sodium channels. Know maximum safe doses (lidocaine ~4.5 mg/kg, or ~7 mg/kg with epinephrine) and signs of systemic toxicity (perioral numbness, seizures, cardiac arrest) treated with lipid emulsion.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka lidocaine, bupivacaine}
Tranexamic Acid [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Antifibrinolytic lysine analogue that inhibits plasminogen activation, reducing perioperative and mucosal (e.g., epistaxis, post-tonsillectomy) bleeding. Caution in thromboembolic risk and seizure history.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka TXA}
Vancomycin [drug · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Glycopeptide covering MRSA and resistant gram-positives via cell-wall synthesis inhibition. Requires trough/AUC monitoring; nephrotoxicity and red-man (infusion) reaction are key adverse effects. Added when MRSA is suspected in severe head and neck infection.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)
Deep Neck Space Abscess [pathology · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · draft] — Suppurative collection within a deep neck space, typically polymicrobial (Streptococcus, anaerobes, Staphylococcus). Diagnosed by contrast CT (ring-enhancing, hypodense center); managed with airway control, IV antibiotics and incision and drainage. Complications include descending mediastinitis, carotid blowout and Lemierre's.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka deep neck infection, retropharyngeal abscess, parapharyngeal abscess}
Descending Necrotizing Mediastinitis [pathology · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · draft] — Life-threatening spread of a deep neck infection into the mediastinum, most often via the retropharyngeal/danger space. High mortality; requires early CT chest, broad-spectrum antibiotics and aggressive surgical drainage (often thoracic).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka mediastinitis}
Ludwig's Angina [pathology · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · draft] — Rapidly spreading bilateral cellulitis of the submandibular (sublingual and submaxillary) spaces, usually of odontogenic origin (2nd/3rd molars). Causes a brawny, non-fluctuant floor-of-mouth induration with tongue elevation and airway compromise; a surgical/airway emergency.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka Ludwig angina}
Von Willebrand Disease [pathology · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Most common inherited bleeding disorder; deficient/defective vWF impairs platelet adhesion and stabilization of factor VIII. Presents with mucocutaneous bleeding and prolonged bleeding after tonsillectomy/adenoidectomy; treated with desmopressin (DDAVP) or vWF concentrate.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka vWD}
Blood Transfusion [procedure · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Replacement of blood products; a restrictive threshold (~Hb 7 g/dL, higher in cardiac disease) is generally favored. Complications include acute/delayed hemolytic reactions, febrile non-hemolytic reactions, TRALI, TACO and infection.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka transfusion, pRBC transfusion}
Cricothyrotomy [procedure · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · midline · draft] — Emergency front-of-neck airway through the cricothyroid membrane for 'cannot intubate, cannot oxygenate' situations. Faster and safer than emergency tracheostomy; converted to a formal tracheostomy within 24–72 hours to avoid subglottic stenosis. Avoid in children under ~12.  (Cummings Otolaryngology; ASA Difficult Airway Guidelines)  {aka cricothyroidotomy, surgical airway}
Endotracheal Intubation [procedure · Fundamentals/Anatomy · draft] — Placement of a cuffed tube into the trachea for airway protection and ventilation, via direct or video laryngoscopy, or awake fiberoptic when difficulty is anticipated. Mallampati, thyromental distance and mouth opening predict difficulty.  (AAO-HNSF Core Curriculum)  {aka intubation, ETT placement}
Incision and Drainage of Neck Abscess [procedure · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · draft] — Surgical drainage of a deep neck abscess through a transcervical or transoral approach after securing the airway. Cultures guide antibiotic de-escalation; small (<2–2.5 cm) collections may resolve with IV antibiotics alone.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka I&D, neck drainage}
Tracheostomy [procedure · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · midline · draft] — Surgical airway created typically between the 2nd and 3rd tracheal rings for prolonged ventilation, upper airway obstruction, or pulmonary toilet. Avoid the thyroid isthmus and high innominate artery; early complications include bleeding and tube dislodgement, late include tracheoinnominate fistula and stenosis.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka tracheotomy}
Ascending Pharyngeal Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Small posteromedial ECA branch (the only medial branch), often arising near the bifurcation. Supplies the pharynx, prevertebral muscles, and meninges; a frequent feeder of jugulotympanic paragangliomas and a target during preoperative embolization.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Carotid Bifurcation [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Division of the common carotid into internal and external carotid arteries, typically at C3–C4 (upper thyroid cartilage). Site of the carotid body (chemoreceptor, origin of carotid body paraganglioma splaying the vessels in a 'lyre sign') and carotid sinus (baroreceptor). Key landmark in neck dissection and carotid endarterectomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Common Carotid Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Right arises from brachiocephalic trunk, left directly from the aortic arch. Ascends within the carotid sheath with no branches until it bifurcates near the superior border of the thyroid cartilage (C3–C4). Digital compression against the carotid (Chassaignac) tubercle of C6 is a temporizing maneuver for control.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka CCA}
Descending Palatine Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Pterygopalatine branch of the maxillary artery descending the greater palatine canal to become the greater palatine artery at the hard palate. Encountered in Le Fort I down-fracture and cleft/palatal surgery; supplies the palate and contributes to the nasal septal (Kiesselbach) plexus.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka greater palatine artery}
External Carotid Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Anteromedial branch of the bifurcation supplying the face, scalp, and neck via eight branches. Distinguished from the ICA by its cervical branches (the ICA has none in the neck). Ligation for hemorrhage should be placed distal to the superior thyroid artery to reduce collateral steal and preserve flow to distal branches.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka ECA}
External Jugular Vein [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Formed by the posterior division of the retromandibular vein and the posterior auricular vein; crosses the sternocleidomastoid superficially to drain into the subclavian vein. Superficial landmark encountered raising a cervical skin flap; drains the scalp and face.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka EJV}
Facial Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Third anterior ECA branch that grooves the posterior submandibular gland before crossing the mandibular border anterior to the masseter (palpable pulse). Ligated during level I neck dissection and submandibular gland excision; marks the facial vein and the marginal mandibular nerve, which lies superficial to it.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka external maxillary artery}
Facial Vein [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Drains the anterior face; joins the anterior division of the retromandibular vein to form the common facial vein, which empties into the internal jugular. Communicates with the cavernous sinus via the ophthalmic veins (valveless), a route for retrograde facial-infection spread. Its crossing of the mandible marks the marginal mandibular nerve.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka common facial vein}
ICA — Cavernous Segment [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · skull-base · paired · draft] — C4 segment coursing through the cavernous sinus in an S-shaped siphon, adjacent to CN III, IV, V1, V2, and VI. A key landmark and danger point lateral to the sphenoid sinus in endoscopic skull-base surgery; dehiscent bony coverage is common.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka carotid siphon}
ICA — Cervical Segment [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — C1 segment from the bifurcation to the carotid canal, giving no branches. Lies in the carotid sheath; a medialized (retropharyngeal/aberrant) course is a hazard during tonsillectomy and adenoidectomy.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
ICA — Petrous Segment [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · skull-base · paired · draft] — C2 segment traversing the carotid canal of the temporal bone with vertical then horizontal (genu) portions. Lies medial to the eustachian tube and anteroinferior to the cochlea — at risk in petrous apex and infratemporal/skull-base approaches.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
ICA — Supraclinoid Segment [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · skull-base · paired · draft] — C6–C7 intradural segment above the anterior clinoid giving the ophthalmic artery (source of the ethmoidal arteries), then terminating into the anterior and middle cerebral arteries. Ophthalmic origin explains why posterior epistaxis from ethmoidal arteries arises from the ICA, not the ECA.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Inferior Alveolar Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — First-part (mandibular) branch of the maxillary artery entering the mandibular foramen with the inferior alveolar nerve to supply the lower teeth. A source of brisk bleeding in mandibular osteotomy, sagittal split, and body/angle fracture fixation.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Lingual Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Second anterior ECA branch supplying the tongue and floor of mouth. Passes deep to hyoglossus; loops within the submandibular triangle where it is a landmark in transoral base-of-tongue surgery (TORS) and can be ligated for tongue hemorrhage. Hyoglossus separates the artery (deep) from the hypoglossal nerve (superficial).  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Lingual Vein [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Drains the tongue and floor of mouth; the accompanying vein (vena comitans of the hypoglossal nerve) and dorsal lingual veins empty into the internal jugular. Encountered during glossectomy and floor-of-mouth resection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Maxillary Artery (Internal Maxillary) [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Larger terminal ECA branch arising deep to the mandibular neck in the infratemporal fossa. Passes into the pterygopalatine fossa and supplies the deep face, nasal cavity, and dura. Principal artery of posterior epistaxis (via sphenopalatine) and a control point in transantral ligation and JNA surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka internal maxillary artery, IMAX}
Occipital Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Posterior ECA branch supplying the posterior scalp. Crosses superficial to the internal carotid and internal jugular and gives the sternocleidomastoid branch, under which the hypoglossal nerve loops — a bleeding pitfall when mobilizing CN XII in upper neck dissection.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Posterior Auricular Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · external-ear · paired · draft] — Small posterior ECA branch supplying the auricle, postauricular scalp, and (via the stylomastoid branch) the facial nerve within the fallopian canal. Basis of postauricular/retroauricular flaps and a source of bleeding during mastoid and parotid surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Pterygoid Venous Plexus [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Valveless venous network in the infratemporal fossa around the lateral pterygoid, draining to the maxillary/retromandibular vein and communicating with the cavernous sinus. A source of troublesome venous bleeding in infratemporal fossa and maxillary surgery and a route of infection to the cavernous sinus.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Superficial Temporal Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · facial-skeleton · paired · draft] — Smaller terminal ECA branch ascending anterior to the tragus (palpable preauricular pulse) into the temporoparietal scalp. Biopsy target in giant cell arteritis, axis of the temporoparietal fascia flap, and a landmark exiting the superior parotid.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)  {aka STA}
Thyrocervical Trunk [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · neck · paired · draft] — Short branch of the first part of the subclavian artery giving the inferior thyroid, transverse cervical, and suprascapular arteries. Origin landmark at the medial supraclavicular fossa in low neck dissection and central compartment surgery.  (Cummings Otolaryngology)
Vertebral Artery [vessel · Fundamentals/Anatomy · cervical-spine-hyoid · paired · draft] — First branch of the subclavian artery; ascends through the cervical transverse foramina (C6 up to C1), enters the foramen magnum, and joins to form the basilar artery. At risk in prevertebral and lateral neck dissection.  (Moore Clinically Oriented Anatomy)
